Stop Passing Paragraphs Between Your Agents

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you've built a multi-step agent workflow, you've probably seen this. Step one does its job and returns a nice paragraph. Step two reads that paragraph and does its job. It all works in testing. Then someone tweaks the first prompt, the paragraph comes out a little differently, and step two starts doing something slightly wrong. No error, no crash. Just quietly worse results.

The problem isn't the model. It's that the contract between your steps is prose, and prose has no shape you can check.

The fix is boring and it works: make each step return structured data, and validate it before the next step runs.

Here's what that looks like with Pydantic:

Most model APIs and agent frameworks now let you ask for output that matches a schema directly. CrewAI has output_pydantic on tasks, and the major providers have structured or JSON output modes. The validation step is still worth keeping, because "supports a schema" and "always returns valid data" aren't quite the same thing.

A few things that make this work well in practice.

Only include fields the next step actually uses. It's tempting to ask for everything, but every extra field is one more thing the model can get wrong and one more thing to keep in sync.

Use Literal types wherever you can. "urgent" vs "Urgent" vs "high priority" is exactly the kind of drift that breaks routing logic downstream.

Let it fail loudly. If a required field is missing, stop right there with a clear error. A failure at step one with a good message is far easier to debug than a weird result at step four.

Retry once, not forever. One retry with the validation error included in the prompt fixes a lot of cases. If it fails twice, send it to a person or a dead-letter queue instead of looping.

Keep a summary field for humans. The structured fields are for your code. A short free-text summary is still useful in logs and for whoever reviews flagged items.

The side benefit is testing. Once each step returns a typed object, you can write normal assertions against it. A small set of fixed inputs with expected fields turns "does the agent still work after my prompt change?" from a vibe check into a test run.

None of this is new. It's the same reason we use typed function signatures instead of passing strings around. Agents just make it easy to forget.

We build AI agents for businesses at Binate AI, and typed handoffs between steps are one of the first things we put in place.

How to Build a Modern Website That Is Fast, Responsive, and SEO-Friendly

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Building a modern website requires more than choosing an attractive design or installing a theme. A successful website should provide a good user experience, load quickly, work across different devices, and make its content easy for search engines to understand.

Developers often focus on visual design first and consider performance and SEO later. However, these elements should be part of the development process from the beginning.

In this tutorial, we'll explore how to build a better website using HTML, CSS, JavaScript, and practical SEO techniques. We'll also look at code examples that can be adapted to WordPress and other website development projects.

HTML provides the foundation of every website. Using semantic HTML elements makes your code easier to maintain and helps browsers and assistive technologies understand your content.

Instead of using generic containers for everything, use elements such as header, nav, main, section, and footer.

Notice that the page has one primary heading and a logical structure. This makes the content easier to navigate and maintain.

A website should work properly on desktop computers, tablets, and smartphones. Responsive design allows the layout to adjust automatically based on the available screen size.

CSS Grid and Flexbox are useful tools for building responsive layouts without depending on complicated frameworks.

The auto-fit and minmax() combination allows the browser to create columns according to the available space.

This approach is particularly useful when building service pages, portfolios, product sections, and business websites.

Website performance affects how visitors interact with a page. A slow website can make navigation frustrating, especially for people using mobile devices or slower internet connections.

Here are several development practices worth considering:

For example, images can use native lazy loading:

For images that appear immediately when the page loads, avoid lazy loading them unnecessarily. Important above-the-fold images may need higher loading priority.

Developers should also measure performance rather than assume that a website is fast. Tools such as Lighthouse and PageSpeed Insights can help identify performance issues.

JavaScript can make a website more interactive, but it should be used carefully. Adding too many scripts can increase page loading time and create unnecessary complexity.

For example, consider a simple mobile navigation menu:

This example uses a button with an accessible expanded state. It also avoids loading an additional JavaScript library for a small interaction.

When developing interactive features, consider keyboard navigation, screen readers, and users who may not have JavaScript enabled.

Search engine optimization should not be treated as a task that happens only after a website is finished.

Technical SEO starts with the website's structure, internal navigation, page accessibility, and crawlability.

Descriptive page titles: Every important page should have a unique title that accurately describes its content.

Useful meta descriptions: Write natural descriptions that explain what visitors can expect from each page.

Logical heading structure: Use headings to organize information instead of selecting heading levels only for visual appearance.

Descriptive URLs: Use readable URLs that clearly communicate the subject of a page.

Internal links: Connect related pages using meaningful anchor text so visitors can discover additional information.

Image descriptions: Add meaningful alternative text to informative images.

Developers should also verify that important pages are not accidentally blocked by robots.txt or marked with an incorrect noindex directive.

For businesses operating in competitive local markets, these technical foundations can be combined with local keyword research, Google Business Profile optimization, on-page SEO, and relevant website content. Businesses looking to improve their visibility can explore professional Milwaukee SEO services as an example of a location-focused SEO approach.

WordPress is a popular content management system because it provides flexibility for developers, businesses, and content creators.

However, installing WordPress does not automatically create a fast or well-optimized website.

A better development workflow includes choosing a reliable hosting environment, using a lightweight theme, limiting unnecessary plugins, and maintaining a clean database.

When developing custom WordPress functionality, use the platform's built-in APIs instead of adding unnecessary code directly to theme files.

For example, WordPress provides a proper method for loading stylesheets:

This approach allows WordPress to manage the stylesheet correctly and helps prevent duplicate or conflicting asset loading.

For larger projects, developers should also consider child themes, custom post types, reusable components, backups, security updates, and staging environments.

A website should be tested before it becomes publicly available. A page that looks perfect on a developer's computer may have problems on other browsers, screen sizes, or devices.

For website redesign projects, URL management is particularly important. If an existing page changes its URL, a relevant permanent 301 redirect should be configured when appropriate.

This helps visitors and search engines reach the correct replacement page instead of encountering a broken link.

Businesses interested in how professional website development, SEO, and digital marketing work together can also read this USA TODAY press release about SEO and web design services in Milwaukee and across Wisconsin.

Building a modern website means bringing design, development, performance, accessibility, and SEO together.

A clean HTML structure, responsive CSS, efficient JavaScript, optimized images, and properly configured WordPress environment can provide a strong foundation for a website.

The most important lesson is to consider these factors during development instead of waiting until the website is finished.

Whether you're creating a new project or redesigning an existing website, focus on building something that is useful, fast, accessible, and easy to maintain.

Good website development is not simply about making a page look attractive. It is about creating a reliable experience that works for real people.

SQLRPGLE: embedded SQL done properly

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 28 • Originally published at rainierio.dev

Embedded SQL in RPG is the default way to access Db2 for i in new code. It is also where I most often see sloppy code: no status checks, SELECT *, and cursors left open. This article gives a pattern you can paste into a real program.

SQL statements start with exec sql and end with a semicolon, and can span lines. The source member type is SQLRPGLE.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

Fixed format has no exec sql ...; one-liner: the statement sits between C/EXEC SQL and C/END-EXEC on C+ lines (/ in column 7, EXEC SQL in columns 8-16). SET OPTION goes there like any other statement.

SET OPTION is not executable; the precompiler reads it. IBM requires it to precede every other SQL statement in the source, and you can code only one, so put all options in that one statement. It lets the source carry its own compile options.

Prefix RPG variables with a colon inside SQL.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

A host structure maps columns by position, not name, so the select list order must match the structure. Always list columns explicitly.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

If no row matches you get SQLSTATE 02000 (SQLCODE 100). If more than one matches, you get an error (SQLSTATE 21000, SQLCODE -811) unless you add fetch first row only. Decide which you mean. A null in a column needs a null-indicator variable (:cust.balance :balInd) or you get SQLCODE -305.

The precompiler gives you sqlstate and sqlcode automatically. Check after every statement. SQLSTATE is the portable value; SQLCODE is Db2-specific but handy.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

DSPLY needs a variable in fixed format, hence MSG. DSPLY also cuts the message at 52 characters, so keep it short. If CHECKSQL is called earlier in the same source, add a D CHECKSQL PR N prototype first.

Warnings start with 01, not found is 02, and 00 is success. Anything else (class 22, 23, 42 and so on) is an error you must handle. For details use GET DIAGNOSTICS CONDITION 1 :msg = MESSAGE_TEXT (the CONDITION 1 is required for condition items like MESSAGE_TEXT, RETURNED_SQLSTATE and DB2_MESSAGE_ID). Capture sqlstate into a variable before you run it, or any other statement, because every later SQL statement can overwrite what you were about to test.

Use a cursor when you expect many rows: declare, open, fetch in a loop, close.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

DECLARE is not executable, but it must appear in the source before the OPEN that uses it, and the host variables it references must be declared before it. closqlcsr(*endmod) closes cursors when the module ends. The ILE default is *ENDACTGRP, so a cursor you forget to close stays open until the activation group ends, and the next call's OPEN fails with SQLSTATE 24502 (already open). Close cursors yourself anyway.

Fetching a block of rows in one call reduces overhead. Use an array data structure.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

SQLERRD(3) (short name SQLER3) holds the number of rows fetched. The last block is usually short: Db2 then returns SQLSTATE 02000 (SQLCODE 100) and the rows it did find, so process SQLERRD(3) rows first and stop afterwards. Reading it straight after the FETCH needs no extra statement; GET DIAGNOSTICS :fetched = ROW_COUNT gives the same count but is one more statement to get wrong.

When the statement text is built at run time, use PREPARE and EXECUTE. Use parameter markers (?), never concatenated user input, to avoid SQL injection.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

The statement literal is split with + because a single literal would run past column 80.

For a one-off statement without parameters, EXECUTE IMMEDIATE :stmt is enough. A dynamic SELECT is prepared, then used with DECLARE CURSOR FOR s1 and OPEN c USING :value.

With COMMIT(*NONE) every statement is permanent immediately. With COMMIT(*CHG) (or *CS, *ALL) changes are held until you issue COMMIT or ROLLBACK.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

Tables must be journaled to be used under commitment control; otherwise the statement fails at runtime (SQLCODE -7008, SQLSTATE 55019). The CRTSQLRPGI default is COMMIT(*CHG), so a program you compile without thinking about it hits this on any unjournaled file. On PUB400 a new SQL schema is journaled automatically, but tables created with CRTPF (see DDS to SQL DDL migration) in a plain library are not.

The precompiler produces a temporary source and then calls the RPG compiler. COMMIT(*NONE) is right for batch utilities (wrap the call in CL and use SBMJOB) and read-only programs; choose *CHG for transactional programs. The SET OPTION line in the source carries the same options with the member.

Defaults worth knowing before you rely on them:

The samples use mylib.customer. With OPTION(*SQL) an unqualified name goes to the default schema instead of the library list.

Same program in fixed format (what you will meet in most shops):

Fixed format wants all D specs before the first C spec, so the declarations move up. SET OPTION is still the first SQL statement in the source, which is what the precompiler needs.

Originally published on rainierio.dev. The full Learn IBM i track, with an Indonesian version, is at rainierio.dev/learn.

How to Self-Host Plex and Jellyfin: A Developer's Field Guide to Your Own Streaming Stack

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 28 • Originally published at mangodeveloper.com

The first time I cancelled Netflix and tried to watch my own ripped movies on a hotel TV, nothing played. The file was H.265 10-bit in MKV. The TV didn't support it. The browser transcoder choked. My "save $20 a month" project turned into a weekend of reading about hardware transcoding, and that's the moment you realize media streaming is genuinely hard. This article is the guide I wish I'd had then.

Plex and Jellyfin both solve the same problem-your media library, served to your devices, with metadata, posters, and a Netflix-like UI. Plex is the polished commercial product with a paywall on the good features. Jellyfin is the AGPL-licensed fork of Emby that doesn't phone home and doesn't ask for a credit card. Most developers I know end up running both for a few weeks and picking a side. I'll show you how to run either (or both) on real hardware, with real transcoding, behind a real reverse proxy, reachable from anywhere.

Both projects are mature, both play every common format, both support hardware transcoding. The differences are about philosophy and friction.

I run Jellyfin in production. I run Plex on a second box for family members whose Smart TVs don't have a Jellyfin app. If your household is mostly Apple TV, Plex is still the safer bet. If everyone's on Roku/Fire Stick/web, Jellyfin is fine and you'll save the Plex Pass fee.

A typical self-hosted media stack looks like this:

┌──────────────────────────┐ │ Internet / Tailscale │ └────────────┬─────────────┘ │ HTTPS ▼ ┌──────────────────────────┐ │ Caddy reverse proxy │ │ (auto TLS via ACME) │ └────────────┬─────────────┘ │ ┌──────────────────┼───────────────────┐ │ │ │ ▼ ▼ ▼ ┌────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ │ Jellyfin │ │ Plex │ │ *arr stack │ │ (Docker) │ │ (Docker) │ │ (Radarr / │ │ │ │ │ │ Sonarr / │ │ /dev/dri │ │ /dev/dri │ │ Prowlarr) │ │ render │ │ render │ │ │ └─────┬──────┘ └──────┬───────┘ └──────┬───────┘ │ │ │ ▼ ▼ ▼ ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │ /mnt/media ← mergerfs( /mnt/disk{1..N} ) │ │ │ │ SnapRAID parity: /mnt/parity (1 disk) │ └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

The key design decisions are: services in Docker, all media mounted read-only inside containers, hardware acceleration passed through /dev/dri, and one reverse proxy handling TLS for everything.

Keep the host layout boring and predictable. Every service gets a config dir under /opt, every persistent dataset under /mnt.

plaintext /opt/jellyfin/ # config, metadata cache, transcode dir /opt/plex/ /opt/caddy/Caddyfile /mnt/disk1/ # individual data disks, mounted at boot /mnt/disk2/ /mnt/parity/ # SnapRAID parity /mnt/media # mergerfs view of disk1..N ├── movies/ ├── tv/ ├── music/ └── photos/

/mnt/media is the only path the containers see. If you ever migrate disks or rebuild the pool, the container mounts don't change.

A single big disk is the easy path until it dies. RAID 5 rebuilds are stressful on modern high-capacity drives and the array is slow while it runs. The homelab consensus is mergerfs (file-level concatenation, no striping) plus SnapRAID (snapshot parity, run nightly).

conf /dev/disk/by-id/ata-WDC_WD40EFRX-1 /mnt/disk1 ext4 defaults,noatime 0 2 /dev/disk/by-id/ata-WDC_WD40EFRX-2 /mnt/disk2 ext4 defaults,noatime 0 2 /dev/disk/by-id/ata-WDC_WD40EFRX-3 /mnt/parity ext4 defaults,noatime 0 2

/mnt/disk1:/mnt/disk2 /mnt/media fuse.mergerfs \ defaults,allow_other,category.create=mfs,moveonenospc=true,dropcacheonrename=true 0 0

conf parity /mnt/parity/snapraid.parity content /mnt/disk1/snapraid.content content /mnt/disk2/snapraid.content content /opt/snapraid/snapraid.content data d1 /mnt/disk1/ data d2 /mnt/disk2/ exclude *.unrecoverable exclude /tmp/ exclude /lost+found/ exclude *.!sync exclude *.bak

set -euo pipefail LOG=/var/log/snapraid.log { echo "=== $(date) ===" snapraid sync snapraid scrub -p 8 -o 1 } >> "$LOG" 2>&1

Crucially: mergerfs is not RAID. If a disk dies before SnapRAID has run, the files written since the last sync are gone. SnapRAID is snapshot parity, not real-time. This is the trade-off. For movies you can re-rip, that's fine. For irreplaceable photos, back them up off-site too.

Direct play is when the client can decode the file as-is. Transcoding is when the server has to convert it on the fly. 4K HEVC to 1080p H.264 in software will pin 8 cores and deliver 4 FPS. The fix is hardware: pass /dev/dri into the container and let the iGPU do it.

vainfo should list VAProfileHEVCMain10 and VAProfileVP9Profile0. If you don't have it, install intel-media-va-driver-non-free on Debian/Ubuntu.

Add your media user to the render group so the container can access the device:

shell sudo usermod -aG render jellyfin sudo usermod -aG render plex

The classical symptom of "transcoding doesn't work" is the server going to 100% CPU and the video pausing every few seconds. That's software transcoding. The container is missing /dev/dri or the render group permission.

Here's a production-ready compose file. Note the /dev/dri device, the render group ID mapped via group_add, and the explicit no-new-privileges security option.

services: jellyfin: image: jellyfin/jellyfin:10.9.11 container_name: jellyfin restart: unless-stopped user: "1000:1000" group_add:

Inside Jellyfin, enable VAAPI under Dashboard → Playback → Transcoding. Pick /dev/dri/renderD128 as the device. Test with a 4K HEVC file forced to 1080p max and a 20 Mbps bitrate cap-CPU should stay under 10%.

Plex is similar but the image expects a claim token from plex.tv on first boot.

services: plex: image: plexinc/pms-docker:latest container_name: plex restart: unless-stopped hostname: plex devices:

Plex Pass is required for hardware transcoding. Without it, you'll see "Transcoding (cpu)" in the dashboard and CPU will spike. Don't waste a weekend debugging the /dev/dri perms if you haven't bought Plex Pass yet-it's gated behind the feature flag.

Plex uses network_mode: host because it broadcasts on multiple ports (32400 main, 32469 DLNA, 1900/5353 UDP for discovery). You can run bridge mode with explicit port mappings, but DLNA auto-discovery will break for clients that rely on it.

You want HTTPS for remote access and you want a single port forwarded. Caddy is the lowest-friction option because ACME is built in.

jellyfin.example.com { reverse_proxy 127.0.0.1:8096 { flush_interval -1 } encode zstd gzip

# Jellyfin's recommended headers header { Strict-Transport-Security "max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains; preload" X-Frame-Options SAMEORIGIN X-Content-Type-Options nosniff Referrer-Policy no-referrer # don't apply Caddy's default CSP, Jellyfin sets its own ?Content-Security-Policy "" }

@websockets { header Connection Upgrade header Upgrade websocket } reverse_proxy @websockets 127.0.0.1:8096 }

plex.example.com { reverse_proxy 127.0.0.1:32400 encode zstd gzip }

The flush_interval -1 is critical for Jellyfin-it disables buffering so streaming chunks reach the client immediately. Without it, you'll see 30-second startup stalls on every video.

services: caddy: image: caddy:2.8-alpine container_name: caddy restart: unless-stopped ports:

If you'd rather not open ports on your router, skip this whole section and use Tailscale.

Cloudflare Tunnel's free ToS technically excludes "large file streaming." People do it anyway and most accounts never get flagged, but if you care about the letter of the agreement, don't. Use Tailscale for family-if they can install a phone app, they can use Tailscale. Use port forwarding + Caddy for yourself.

shell curl -fsSL https://tailscale.com/install.sh | sh sudo tailscale up --advertise-tags=tag:media

On each client device, install Tailscale, log into the same tailnet, and you're done. The Jellyfin URL becomes http://jellyfin-server:8096 from anywhere on the tailnet. No TLS, no port forwarding, no DNS.

Both Plex and Jellyfin have hard opinions about file layout. Get this wrong and metadata matching turns into a part-time job.

plaintext /mnt/media/movies/ The Matrix (1999)/ The Matrix (1999) [1080p] [Bluray].mkv The Matrix Reloaded (2003)/ The Matrix Reloaded (2003) [1080p] [Bluray].mkv

/mnt/media/tv/ Breaking Bad (2008)/ Season 01/ Breaking Bad - S01E01 - Pilot.mkv Breaking Bad - S01E02 - Cat's in the Bag.mkv

/mnt/media/music/ Artist/ Album (Year)/ 01 - Track.flac

The patterns the metadata agents look for:

Avoid subfolders with extras, posters, behind-the-scenes mixed in. Put those in a separate Extras/ folder inside the movie folder-Jellyfin and Plex both special-case it.

Docker containers run as whatever UID/GID you tell them. If the host files are owned by 1000:1000 and you let the container run as root, you'll end up with files owned by root that you can't delete from the host later. Worse, if Jellyfin can't write to its config dir, the wizard silently fails.

Pick a UID/GID and stick with it across every service. Mine is 1000:1000.

shell sudo chown -R 1000:1000 /opt/jellyfin /opt/plex /mnt/media sudo chmod -R 775 /opt/jellyfin /opt/plex sudo find /mnt/media -type d -exec chmod 775 {} \; sudo find /mnt/media -type f -exec chmod 664 {} \;

The render group needs to be the same GID inside and outside the container. Check on the host:

Then in compose, group_add: ["109"] makes the container's process a member of that group. If the GID on the host is different (some distros use 44), change the number.

Prometheus node_exporter for the host, plus Jellyfin's built-in webhook for play events. The minimum useful dashboard tracks:

yaml services: node_exporter: image: prom/node-exporter:v1.8.2 container_name: node_exporter restart: unless-stopped pid: host volumes:

Pair with Grafana and a SMART exporter (smartctl-exporter) and you'll know a drive is failing before it dies. Don't skip this-nothing is worse than explaining to your partner why all the movies vanished on movie night.

Running as root in the container. Symptom: files created on the host array are owned by root and you can't rm them without sudo. Fix: set user: in compose, chown the host directories to match.

Forgetting the render group on the device. Symptom: Jellyfin says "VAAPI init failed" or silently falls back to software. Fix: group_add: [""] and ls -l /dev/dri shows the group can read renderD128.

Exposing Jellyfin directly to the internet without HTTPS. Symptom: login page on HTTP, your password goes in cleartext. Fix: Caddy + ACME, or Tailscale.

Mounting media read-write. Symptom: Jellyfin happily renames your carefully named files based on a bad metadata match. Fix: :ro on the mount. Use a separate /incoming volume if Jellyfin needs to write.

Putting the transcode cache on a spinning disk. Symptom: seeking is slow, transcoding stalls. Fix: transcode to /config/transcode on an SSD, or mount tmpfs.

Trusting one disk with no parity. Symptom: drive dies, library is gone. Fix: SnapRAID parity, plus off-site backup of anything irreplaceable.

Forgetting to update TLS SAN when changing hostname. Symptom: mobile app refuses to connect after a hostname change. Fix: regenerate cert, or use a wildcard.

"Videos buffer every 10 seconds." Open the Jellyfin dashboard while playing. If you see "Direct playing," the network is the bottleneck-your home upload or the client's download. If you see "Transcoding (cpu)," hardware accel isn't working. Check vainfo inside the container:

If vainfo doesn't list HEVC, the wrong driver is loaded. On Intel 12th gen+, you need intel-media-va-driver-non-free (Debian) or intel-media-va-driver (newer versions support all features).

"Plex remote access says 'not available outside your network.'" Plex's relay service uses a stun-style NAT check. If it can't reach plex.tv:32400 from inside, it won't publish. Check docker logs plex | grep -i relay and confirm outbound 32400/TCP works. Sometimes you need to set PLEX_ADVERTISE_URL to your public hostname explicitly.

"Music won't scan, every album shows as unknown." The metadata agent is probably set to TheAudioDB but your folder layout is wrong. Music agents are extremely picky: / ()/NN - .ext. Check Media Info on a single track-if Album and Artist tags are missing from the file itself, fix the tags with beet or MusicBrainz Picard before rescanning.

"Library scan is at 1% after an hour." Either the disk is slow (check iostat -x 1) or you've pointed Jellyfin at the wrong directory and it's walking the entire filesystem. docker exec jellyfin find /media -type f | wc -l should match what you expect. If it's millions of files, you've mounted something you didn't mean to.

"Subtitles don't show." Most clients only support SRT for direct play. PGS or ASS subs force a transcode. Convert with ffmpeg -i input.mkv -map 0:s:0 -c:s srt output.en.srt and put the SRT next to the video.

update: docker compose -f /opt/jellyfin/docker-compose.yml pull docker compose -f /opt/jellyfin/docker-compose.yml up -d docker image prune -f

backup: tar czf /mnt/parity/backups/jellyfin-config-$(date +%F).tgz /opt/jellyfin/config rsync -a /mnt/parity/backups/ user@offsite:/backups/jellyfin/ find /mnt/parity/backups -name 'jellyfin-config-*.tgz' -mtime +30 -delete

shell 0 3 * * 0 root make -C /opt update && make -C /opt backup

For major version upgrades (Plex, Jellyfin), snapshot the config volume first. With ZFS:

shell zfs snapshot tank/jellyfin-config@pre-$(date +%F)

shell lvcreate -s -L 5G -n jellyfin-snap /dev/vg0/jellyfin

If the upgrade breaks something, revert the snapshot. Without a snapshot, you're restoring from tarball and praying the database schema is compatible.

A real breakdown for a 4-disk NUC-based server:

Power draw is the recurring cost. A NUC + 4 spinning disks idle around 35W. At $0.15/kWh, that's about $46/year. You're not saving money in year one. You're saving money in year three, and you own the library.

Family with mixed devices. I run Jellyfin as the primary, Plex for the one Smart TV that doesn't have a Jellyfin app. Same media mount, two services, no duplication.

University dorm media share. A friend runs Jellyfin for his floor with Tailscale ACLs. Each student gets an account, watch history is per-user, and the admin can see who's hammering the transcoder at 2 AM.

Photo backup with auto-tagging. Jellyfin has an image library mode that auto-tags faces using a local ML model. Mount /mnt/media/photos, enable the plugin, let it run overnight. It's not Google Photos, but the photos never leave your network.

Personal music streaming. Jellyfin has a competent music player and integrates with Finamp on mobile. The audio quality beats Spotify's free tier because you control the source files.

Self-hosting your media is one of the few projects that delivers exactly what it promises: your library, on your hardware, on your terms. The setup is genuinely fiddly the first time-permissions, hardware transcoding, reverse proxy, and remote access all want to bite you. Once it works, it stays working for years. The trade-off is your time and the discipline to keep backups. If you're the kind of developer who reads this site, you'll find the fiddliness rewarding rather than annoying.

Generate a Maze With a Guaranteed Solution in Python (API Tutorial)

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A maze generator is easy to write badly. Carve some passages, knock down some walls, ship it — and sooner or later it hands you a maze where the goal is walled off completely. In a game that's an unwinnable level. In a printed activity book, that's a kid discovering the answer key doesn't work, and a refund instead of a review.

So the maze generator I built for my books doesn't just carve — it proves. It walks the finished maze from start to goal before anything ships, and the proof travels with the puzzle. This is that generator, exposed as an API called ActivityForge. In this tutorial we'll call it from Python: send a size and a seed, get back a maze, its wall data, and the verified solution path.

The API lives on RapidAPI. The free tier gives you 100 calls a day, and every plan — free included — reaches every endpoint and every format. The paid plans only change how many calls you get, starting at $19/month. Nothing in this tutorial needs a paid plan.

The whole request is one POST. Two headers carry your key and the host; the JSON body carries the maze settings.

A few things worth knowing about the parameters:

The response describes the maze as data, not a picture. Coordinates are [x, y] — x is the column, y is the row — with start at the top-left cell [0, 0] and goal at the bottom-right. The walls field is a list of rows; each cell carries four booleans, top, right, bottom, and left, saying which of its walls stand. That's everything a renderer needs:

That's a real response, not a mockup — seed 42 will hand you this exact maze every time.

Here's the part that makes the maze safe to ship. Alongside the walls, the response carries a solution object: path (the route as [x, y] cells, start to goal), directions (the same route as words — right, down, left, up), and steps (the move count).

The generator builds a perfect maze — a spanning tree over the grid, so exactly one route joins any two cells — then breadth-first walks that route before returning anything. The solution is the route it walked, so it can't disagree with the walls. Feed the path back into the renderer as marked cells and the proof draws itself:

Trace the stars yourself: every step moves through a gap, never through a wall, from the S in the top-left to the G in the bottom-right. Generating levels? That directions list doubles as a walkthrough your game can replay. Printing? It's the answer key.

Because we asked for format: "all", the same response also carries svg (a print-ready page) and pdf_base64 (a vector PDF, base64-encoded). Saving the PDF is three lines:

That's the page you hand to a kid — titled, with "Find your way from START to GOAL!" on it, no drawing required.

Look back at the request: "seed": 42. Run it tomorrow, next month, on a different machine — same maze, wall for wall, same 38-step solution. Leave the seed out and every call returns a fresh random maze. For print: generate a catalog once, record the seeds, re-render identical puzzles forever. For a game: a daily maze that's identical for every player, from one integer.

The maze is one of four generators, all with the same request shape and the same seed behavior:

Every generator proves its own output the same way — word placements re-checked against the finished grid, dot sequences checked complete and ordered — because a broken puzzle in a printed book is a refund, not a bug report.

The free tier is 100 calls a day, which is plenty to build something real. The word-search script from the previous tutorial, plus a JavaScript version, is in the examples repo, and the API itself is on RapidAPI.

If you generate a maze pack or a level set with it, I'd like to see what you made.

Part of the ActivityForge series — the previous tutorial builds a printable word search with its answer key, and the launch post covers how these generators came out of my activity-book pipeline.

The Black Friday deals have arrived! Set yourself up with a turkey sandwich and a warm drink, because it's time to shop the best sale event of the year. And after the year we've had, we could all use a few bargains to cheer us up, score that upgrade we've been needing, and get a few gifts in the bag.

The Reviews team at WIRED tracks prices on our favorite gadgets year-round, and we highlight deals only on devices we’ve tested and would recommend to our own friends and family. These are the discounts that caught our eye—vetted for price hikes and fake deals. And if you’re looking for something more distinctive and gift-worthy, we’ve curated some special finds on sale.

Nvidia Stock Is Still Down 38% From Its Peak Despite Jensen Huang's Return. History Suggests a $10,000 Investment Today Will Be Worth This Much by Year-End 2027.

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia's accelerating earnings growth and historically cheap valuation could help investors double their money by the end of 2027.

By Adam Spatacco – Nov 27, 2026 at 7:51PM EST

On Nov. 23, Nvidia (NVDA +0.80%) announced that co-founder Jensen Huang had been reinstated as the company's chief executive officer. Although Nvidia stock initially soared following the announcement, shares have subsequently surrendered some of those gains and remain approximately 38% below their all-time high from October.

Interestingly, Nvidia's recent sell-off comes at a time when the company's financial outlook is becoming even more encouraging. Between accelerating revenue and earnings growth, an enormous share repurchase program, and a historically inexpensive valuation, Nvidia looks positioned for an incredible comeback.

Let's explore how much a $10,000 investment in Nvidia stock today could be worth by the end of 2027.

During the third quarter of fiscal 2027 (ended Oct. 25), Nvidia generated $115.8 billion in revenue, representing 103% growth year over year. Meanwhile, adjusted earnings per share (EPS) increased by 111% to $2.68. Moreover, management raised its fiscal 2028 revenue guidance from 70% to 75%.

Assuming Nvidia meets its fourth-quarter revenue outlook of $130 billion, the company should finish fiscal 2027 with roughly $424 billion in sales. This implies revenue approaching $742 billion next year.

While Nvidia continues to benefit from robust demand for its Blackwell chip architecture, the commercialization of its Vera Rubin architecture has become the company's next major growth catalyst. Nvidia's latest computing platform incorporates graphics processing units (GPUs), Vera central processing units (CPUs), and advanced networking technologies.

This combination broadens Nvidia's opportunities to monetize different layers of the artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure value chain. As hyperscalers continue investing in data center expansion, Nvidia's comprehensive product portfolio provides the company with multiple avenues to sustain its growth.

In September, Nvidia's board of directors increased the company's share repurchase authorization by $150 billion. As of the end of October, Nvidia had $227.3 billion remaining under its buyback program. Management intends to execute the buybacks by the end of fiscal 2028.

Interestingly, Nvidia's recent share-price depreciation could make these repurchases more accretive to shareholders. Even if net income remained flat, reducing the outstanding share count should increase Nvidia's EPS considerably.

Over the last five years, Nvidia has traded at an average forward price-to-earnings (P/E) multiple of approximately 32. Following its recent sell-off, Nvidia is trading at roughly 8.9 times estimated earnings over the next 12 months.

Given Nvidia's enormous size, I don't expect the company to reclaim its historical average valuation profile. However, even modest multiple expansion could generate multibagger returns.

By the end of next year, investors will be valuing Nvidia based on its fiscal 2029 earnings potential. Currently, Wall Street expects Nvidia to generate normalized EPS of approximately $21.65 in fiscal 2029. Applying a forward P/E multiple of 15 to this earnings estimate implies a stock price of roughly $325.

NVDA EPS Estimates for Current Fiscal Year data by YCharts

Using Nvidia's recent share price of $149, a $325 price target implies 118% upside. This would transform a $10,000 investment into roughly $21,800 in just one year.

Given Nvidia's accelerating earnings potential and historically compressed valuation, the company's recent sell-off could represent a lucrative opportunity to buy the dip for patient investors.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 777%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 199% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

*Stock Advisor returns as of November 27, 2026.

Adam Spatacco has positions in Nvidia. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Nvidia. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Israeli bank lets customers buy Bitcoin but charges them 2% to get in and 2% to get out

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Israel Discount Bank offers Bitcoin and Ethereum trading with a 2% commission per trade and restrictions on external transfers.

Israel Discount Bank opened Bitcoin and Ethereum trading in its retail banking app on Nov. 26, letting eligible customers buy, hold, and sell the two assets through their securities account.

The service blocks transfers to private wallets and outside institutions. To cash out, customers sell in the app, and the bank credits US dollars to their account.

The bank’s product FAQ says the service is available to Israeli residents aged 18 and older who have signed up for securities trading with Discount Bank.

Customers then accept a separate crypto trading agreement in the app to place orders through the existing account and see the holdings alongside their other investments.

Customers who already hold Bitcoin or Ethereum elsewhere can’t bring those coins into the service, so the account serves as both the place to trade and the place where customers see their crypto holdings.

The bank credits sale proceeds in US dollars the next business day, and customers who want shekels or another currency can ask the bank to convert them.

Bitcoin and Ether stay inside the service. Customers sell in the app to receive dollars in their bank account.

Selling costs 2% before any currency conversion charge and credits U.S. dollars on the next business day.

Terms apply to Discount Bank’s service. The 2% sale charge excludes any applicable currency conversion fee. Cash settles on the next business day after a completed trade.

Maps the routes described in Discount Bank’s FAQ. The solid line traces a sale and dollar settlement. The dashed branch marks the external crypto transfer restriction.

Terms apply to Discount Bank’s service. The 2% sale charge excludes any applicable currency conversion fee. Cash settles on the next business day after a completed trade.

The bank lists dealing hours of 9 a.m. to 11 p.m.; the app accepts orders for immediate execution only during those hours. Market conditions, insufficient liquidity, blockchain congestion, or technical problems can limit execution.

Please enable JavaScript to verify and send this form.

The bank’s Nov. 11 announcement said it holds customer assets in bank wallets that use Fireblocks infrastructure. The FAQ says licensed third parties provide the service and custody.

Discount Bank waives custody fees for six months after the Nov. 26 launch, then lists a custody fee of 0.25% per quarter.

Trading carries a separate 2% charge on each buy and sell. Customers who buy and later sell pay that commission on both transactions, with each charge based on the transaction value.

For customers who want to convert the resulting dollars into shekels, any applicable currency conversion fee is added to the sale commission.

Vanguard's Growth ETF Has 3 Stocks Making Up More Than a Third of It. Only 1 Has Held Up in the Tech Sell-Off.

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The stock that's held up best belongs to the slowest grower of the three.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 27, 2026 at 7:49PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Vanguard Morningstar Growth ETF ›

A big part of the Vanguard Morningstar Growth ETF (VUG +2.06%) comes down to three companies. At the end of October, Apple (AAPL +0.83%) made up 13.7% of the fund's assets, Nvidia (NVDA +0.80%) 12.8%, and Microsoft (MSFT +0.60%) 10.5%. Together, they were around 37% of a fund that owns over 130 stocks.

And the past seven weeks have been tough for the fund. Its record close was on Oct. 6, at $92.64. By Nov. 25, it had lost around 15%, to $79.10. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite has also slid around 15% from its Oct. 6 high. The S&P 500 is down around 8% over the same stretch.

But the fund's three giants haven't fallen together. Apple is the only one of them that's climbed since Oct. 6. Its stock has gained around 7% over that time, closing Nov. 25 at $355.58, within a dollar of its Nov. 13 record.

Microsoft has dipped around 7%, a milder fall than the fund's.

Nvidia, meanwhile, has plunged about 38%.

Nvidia's slump started in October. Shares were down around 15% from their record by Oct. 30, and dropped further after Nvidia announced Jensen Huang's resignation as CEO on Nov. 12.

Nvidia announced his reinstatement on Nov. 23. Still, Nvidia closed Nov. 25 at $149.30, well below its $239.24 record on Oct. 6.

But the chipmaker's sales are still booming. For Nvidia's fiscal third quarter of 2027, which ended Oct. 25, 2026, revenue grew 103% year over year to $115.8 billion. That was also a gain of 20% from the quarter before. And management guided for around $130 billion in sales for the fiscal fourth quarter, which includes an extra week.

Microsoft's stock hit a record on Nov. 4, closing at $555.80, and it finished Nov. 25 around 12% under that peak. But in the software giant's first quarter of fiscal 2027 (the period ended Sept. 30, 2026), revenue climbed 19% to $92.3 billion, and revenue from its Azure cloud platform surged 48%.

Apple's latest numbers were solid, too. Revenue in its fiscal fourth quarter of 2026, which ended Sept. 26, rose 12% year over year to $114.5 billion, a record for a September quarter. iPhone revenue grew 17% to $57.6 billion, and services revenue hit an all-time high of $31.6 billion.

The iPhone maker's growth, though, is cooling. For the June quarter (Apple's fiscal third quarter), revenue rose 16%. Then growth eased to 12% in the September quarter. This leaves Apple the slowest grower of the three, trailing Microsoft's 19% and Nvidia's 103%.

On the earnings call, CFO Kevan Parekh guided for revenue growth of 7% to 10% for the December quarter. He said the outlook reflects the company's best view of tight supply. By his account, then, the limit is supply, not demand.

He also expects gross margin to be between 45% and 46%, and he pointed mostly to higher memory costs. That compares with 47.9% for the September quarter, which included about a percentage point of lift from tariff refunds.

"What we're seeing with memory pricing right now is unprecedented," CEO John Ternus said during the call.

The fund owns every stock in around the same proportion as its weighting in the index it tracks, and these weights mostly shift with share prices. It doesn't sell what held up to buy more of what dropped.

By my rough math, using Nov. 25 closing prices, Apple's weight has probably risen to around 15%, while Microsoft stands near 11% and Nvidia near 10%. The three still add up to more than a third of the fund. But the fund now leans harder on Apple than it did at the end of October.

This matters because Apple is also the priciest of the three. Its stock sells for around 40 times earnings, compared with about 16 times earnings for Nvidia. In other words, investors pay about two and a half times as much for every dollar of Apple's profit.

To the company's credit, Apple's revenue reached a record $479 billion for fiscal 2026, and its iPhone sales are still rising. But for those holding the fund into 2027, I think the bigger risk now is with Apple, not Nvidia. If Apple's growth keeps slowing while its memory costs rise, a stock at 40 times earnings might have room to fall. And the fund has more of it than it did in October.

$100 Off the AirPods Pro 3 Might Just Be the Best Black Friday Deal This Year

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, Rolling Stone may receive an affiliate commission.

This year’s Black Friday has brought some steep savings on headphones, tech, and more. And while we’ve done our best to comb through the thousands of deals to find the ones that are actually worth it, if you’re looking for just one deal, you could do a lot worse than a $100 discount on the latest AirPods Pro.

Currently, the third-generation AirPods Pro are available for $149, a 40% discount on the regular $249 price. This deal is available on Amazon and other retailers, including Target and Walmart. Apple, meanwhile, is offering a $50 gift card with a purchase of the AirPods Pro 3.

→ Built-in heart rate sensor→ Live-translation→ Hearing protection

When we tested Apple’s AirPods, we were impressed by the wide variety of features beyond listening to music. They feature a built-in heart rate monitor, which is unobtrusive and impressively accurate. They can also be used for hearing protection in loud settings or as a hearing aid for mild to moderate hearing loss.

The AirPods Pro 3 have among the best noise cancellation on the market, if one or two steps behind Bose’s QuietComfort Ultra earbuds. The audio quality itself is impressive, with a clear, neutral sound profile across a range of genres.

The earbuds feature pinch and swipe controls on the stems — they’re easy to use, and the sensors’ discreet placement makes accidentally touching them less likely while adjusting the fit. Apple slightly redesigned the shape of the earbuds so they fit more securely, and the shorter stems don’t stick out as much. I found it easy to get a good fit out of the box, and Apple includes a total of five eartips for a custom fit.

Unsurprisingly, the AirPods work very well with Apple devices, but their features are limited if you’re using an Android device. Fortunately, there are other deals as well, including discounts on the Pixel Buds Pro 2 and Samsung’s Galaxy Buds4 Pro.

Accenture vs. Intuit: Which Technology Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Accenture serves about 75% of the Fortune Global 100, while Intuit's 93 million customers span TurboTax and QuickBooks. Valuation and growth tell very different stories.

By Sara Appino – Updated Nov 27, 2026 at 7:46PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Accenture Plc ›

As digital transformation and artificial intelligence redefine corporate operations and personal finance, choosing between service giant Accenture (ACN +0.56%) and software leader Intuit (INTU +0.08%) represents a fundamental choice for investors.

Accenture focuses on global enterprise consulting and technology implementation, while Intuit dominates the tax and accounting software market for individuals and small businesses. Both companies leverage massive data sets to improve their platforms, but they operate with different growth profiles and capital structures.

Accenture serves approximately 9,000 clients, including about 75% of the Fortune Global 100 and 500 companies. This broad reach across the tech stocks landscape is supported by 18 key technology ecosystem partners that generate around 65% of its total revenue. The company maintains deep roots with its clients, as 85% of its top 200 commercial partners have worked with the firm for 20 years or more.

In the 12 months ended Aug. 31, 2026, Accenture reported revenue of about $74.2 billion, representing 6.5% growth compared to the prior year. The company delivered GAAP net income of approximately $8.4 billion during this period. This steady growth reflects a consistent demand for digital transformation services across its five major industry groups, including communications and financial services.

Accenture maintains a debt-to-equity ratio of about 0.4x, which compares its total debt to shareholder equity. As of its August 2026 balance sheet, the current ratio is approximately 1.4x, indicating it can cover short-term liabilities with short-term assets. In the fiscal year ended Aug. 31, 2026, free cash flow was about $11.6 billion, representing the cash left after capital investments.

Intuit serves about 93 million customers through its integrated financial technology platform, which includes household names like TurboTax and QuickBooks. The company provides essential tools for tax preparation, small business accounting, and credit monitoring to a global user base. Its strategy focuses on an AI-driven ecosystem that automates business workflows and financial management for consumers and mid-market enterprises.

In the 12 months ended Oct. 31, 2026, Intuit achieved revenue of about $21.9 billion, which marked a 12.8% increase over the previous year. The company generated GAAP net income of approximately $4.8 billion during this same period. These results highlight the performance of its financial technology ecosystem, particularly as it expands into larger enterprise markets while maintaining its core consumer base.

Intuit reported a debt-to-equity ratio of about 0.4x, while free cash flow reached about $8.7 billion in the fiscal year ended July 31, 2026. As of its July 2026 balance sheet, the current ratio of approximately 1.5x suggests it can meet short-term obligations. Note that stock-based compensation represented roughly 23.3% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

Accenture faces intense competition from global technology providers like Amazon (AMZN +3.96%), offshore IT firms, and specialized consultancies. The company also manages significant cybersecurity risks, as sophisticated attacks targeting sensitive client data could damage its reputation and financial standing. Additionally, uncertain economic conditions may lead clients to reduce or defer spending on major digital projects.

Intuit faces aggressive competition from both established entities and emerging start-ups, including potential government-funded tax software alternatives. The company also relies on third-party relationships with financial institutions and digital platform stores like those operated by Apple (AAPL +0.83%). Disruptions in these partnerships or shifts in generative AI accuracy could harm operations and customer trust.

Accenture offers a lower P/S ratio, measuring price against sales over the past twelve months, while Intuit features a lower Forward P/E using future earnings estimates.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

I'd go with Intuit. Millions of people and small businesses count on its software every day for taxes and bookkeeping, from TurboTax to QuickBooks, and Intuit has built AI into those tools to handle chores like payroll, paying bills, and sending invoices. What impresses me is the momentum: Sales rose 12% year over year in its latest quarter, and customers can already use QuickBooks right inside popular AI chatbots.

Intuit does have things to work through, though. Its growth is expected to slow to about 8% to 9% this fiscal year. Even so, its AI push gives it room to keep growing.

To be fair to Accenture, it's a giant at helping companies put AI to work, and it returns lots of cash to shareholders. But analysts keep asking whether AI will let clients pay for fewer consulting hours, and Accenture says prices fell in many areas last quarter. Only about 3% of last year's 5% growth came from its own business, and it expects just 3% to 6% growth in the year ahead.

Intuit, meanwhile, is growing faster and turning that growth into even bigger profit gains. For long-term investors, a software leader with momentum like this makes Intuit the better buy for 2027.

One Thing About Prime Video’s Clashing Through The Snow Really Upset Fans (Myself Included)

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elf Is A Holiday Classic Now, But One Star Admits It Was ‘Risky’ Business At The Time

The Internet Can’t Stop Talking About People’s Mouths In Netflix’s The Christmas Switch. I Can’t Unsee It

Why Is Netflix’s Guarding Stars A Christmas Movie When Its Book Is Not? I Asked Jared Padalecki

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The holiday movies have officially taken over the 2026 TV schedule, and I could not be happier. Between Hallmark’s Countdown to Christmas lineup, Netflix’s releases, and even Hulu’s 12 Dates of Christmas, the options have been endless. Now, Prime Video is getting in on the fun with the release of Clashing Through the Snow. As of writing this, the festive flick is sitting in the number three spot on the streamer’s trending charts, but don’t let the popularity fool you. Some fans are really upset that the film featured a character asking ChatGPT for relationship advice, and I agree with their outrage.

The romantic comedy follows rival book editors Hannah (Michelle Randolph) and Ryan (Christopher Briney) who, after learning their jobs were in jeopardy unless they could wow their boss, set out to land major book deals. When weather and a series of misfortunes halt their respective travel plans, the two begrudgingly agree to road trip to New York together. The proximity allows them to bond, and sparks start to fly, complicating their career ambitions.

Clashing Through the Snow is overflowing with witty banter, memorable scenes, and hilarious and endearing side characters, which is why it was so upsetting when Ryan opted to ask ChatGPT for relationship advice instead of calling his friend Walter (Andrew Schulz), who had already given him advice earlier in the film.

Fans were not happy with the unexpected turn and quickly turned to Twitter to air their grievances over the egregious use of AI:

Things weren’t much better over on Threads, where fans also made their distaste for the inclusion abundantly clear:

I don’t know what’s more upsetting: the fact that the film had a Ryan ask ChatGPT for advice or the fact that he was a senior book editor with human friends he could have turned to.

What might be the most infuriating thing is that apparently there was a version of Clashing through the Snow where Ryan did turn to a human instead of ChatGPT. At least that’s what Carlson Young, the film’s director, told Deadline before defending her decision to go with the scene in the film, saying:

I personally thought that him talking to Chat was so Ryan. It made me laugh so much. And the amount of times that I’ve caught my husband… I’m like, ‘Are you asking ChatGPT a question? Like for real? Are you serious? ‘And he is serious. It just felt really modern and so of the times, and something like his character would actually do. And I like a lot the responses that Chat has for him. It just made me laugh.

Maybe I missed something, but I can’t wrap my head around hopeless romantic, pragmatic Ryan being the character who would turn to ChatGPT to ask for serious relationship help. That seems like a job he would have wanted a human who can experience actual love to help with, like Walter or even some random stranger he could have stumbled upon.

As upsetting as it is to see an amazing character turn to AI for help, I don’t think one scene should tarnish the rest of the movie. Clashing Through the Snow is a swoonworthy and hilarious rom-com that does deserve its flowers. Dare I say it might be Briney's best rom-com role (yes, even better than his portrayal as Conrad Fisher in The Summer I Turned Pretty).

Clashing Through the Snow is streaming now for those with an active Prime Video subscription.

Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic put BioWare through "sympathy crunch," says studio veteran

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Prices were correct when checked but can change. We may earn a commission when you buy through these links.

From the creators of Edge: A weekly videogame industry newsletter with analysis from expert writers, guidance from professionals, and insight into what's on the horizon.

Get sneak previews, exclusive competitions and details of special events each month!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"I am supporting all those people that have work to do"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There's often a tragic backstory to any great game in which developers were forced to work way beyond their scheduled hours, often late into nights and even on weekends. Usually, the perception is that everyone's working their tail off to get a project out the door in time for whatever deadline is set by the publisher, but in the case of BioWare's seminal 2003 RPG Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic, a lot of people showed up at the studio just because they felt bad for people who actually had stuff to do.

John Winski, a former employee of BioWare going all the way back to 1996, reflects on Knights of the Old Republic's development in a recent conversation with Pembridge TV. "I think we worked five months of crunch time," he says. "We started with Tuesdays and Thursdays, we'd work 12-hour days. Then it became five days a week working 12-hour days. Saturdays, I don't know how long we were doing seven days a week, 12-hour days."

That's a lot of crunch. Without official numbers to go by, it's hard to know how that stacks up against notoriously crunch-heavy development cycles like The Last of Us 2, Red Dead Redemption 2, and Cyberpunk 2077, but if you're doing the math, that equates to 84-hour work weeks, more than double the average in most places. According to Winski, "a lot of times it was sympathy crunch," meaning workers who weren't required to come into the studio were doing so anyway because they felt bad for those dealing with mandated crunch.

"You don't have any work, but a bunch of other people have to be here, so you're gonna be here too. Which wasn't great, but also, if you look back, it's like, 'I am supporting all those people that have work to do," adds Winski. "Why wouldn't I be? And when you're young, you're kind of stupid and going, 'well, let's just do it', and it worked."

Can't argue with that. I mean, I'll argue all day long against crunch culture, but there's no denying that Knights of the Old Republic was a huge success, becoming an immediate hit and the best-selling Star Wars game of all time when it released. It's also not just one of the best Star Wars games to this day, it's also one of the most beloved RPGs of all time, proving that, unfortunately, great games can come from problematic working environments.

On the other hand, I'm reminded of comments from earlier this year made by Fallout co-creator Tim Cain, who offered a different perspective on crunch in the games industry. While he said he's "glad things have changed" and called Fallout's development "unsustainable," Cain said the developers working unreasonable hours on Fallout were doing so because "they loved what [they] were doing." I'd like to think the Knights of the Old Republic team felt the same.

BioWare turned down Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic 2 because LucasArts wanted the RPG done in 18 months

That means I'm responsible for managing the site's western regional executive branch, AKA my home office, and writing about whatever horror game I'm too afraid to finish.

3 Reasons I Might Buy More Nvidia in December

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The stock is down nearly 30% over the past month, even as its fundamentals have only improved.

By Rick Munarriz – Nov 27, 2026 at 7:38PM EST

I was unfashionably late to Nvidia (NVDA +0.80%) as an investor. These days, I'm unfortunately underwater. I've admired the business for decades, but it's only been eight months since I initiated a stake in the AI bellwether.

With a cost basis in the high $170s, I was doing pretty well through the first few months. The past month has been understandably rough. However, as I size up any portfolio moves I may want to make before heading into 2027, adding to my Nvidia position is on my short list. Let's go over some of the reasons I would be comfortable with buying more Nvidia in December.

I may as well start by addressing the situation with CEO Jensen Huang. Nvidia stock was already under selling pressure before reports surfaced on not just one -- but two -- extramarital affairs. Failing the moral compass test comes with a reputational hit, but it's not necessarily a career killer. Presidents, coaches, and celebrities of all genders who get caught cheating don't automatically lose their high-profile positions.

Both of Huang's adulterous accusations were problematic. Sleeping with an alleged Chinese spy posed a potential national security risk. Having a fling with a direct subordinate is reportedly against Nvidia's own code of ethics. You can't blame Nvidia directors for not simply sweeping it under the boardroom rug.

Letting Huang go wasn't a surprise. Even founder CEOs aren't above a company's code of ethics. Huang hoping for a second chance and the board initially sticking to its druthers were the next obvious chess moves. Things get thorny here because of the political intervention. The White House boasting about pressuring Nvidia's board to take him back isn't going to pass a lot of sniff tests for overreach, but take a step back.

Is the bigger risk here that Nvidia's board is now exposed as toothless, or what a hungry Huang could have done if he started a rival company? Nvidia directors will be fine in the aftermath. This isn't the kind of card a dismissed CEO can play twice, especially now with more eyes on him in social circles. The biggest positive of this very negative situation is that Nvidia and its moat are back at full strength.

A blowout report following a series of downticks is often the elixir that can turn a laggard into a leader. It didn't happen this time. Nvidia exceeded expectations. It boosted guidance for the next fiscal year. It wasn't enough to snap the bearish momentum.

Nvidia and its interim CEO signaled that its prospects were getting brighter. The market still reached for the dimmer.

I can very well be wrong, but looking back, this could be one of those obvious buying opportunities. A market that punishes improving fundamentals for a business that investors were already rotating out of doesn't typically stay down for long.

Nvidia's revenue more than doubled in the quarter it reported 10 days ago. It's now modeling a 91% jump for the current quarter. It boosted its revenue growth outlook for the new fiscal year starting in February from 70% to 75%.

You would expect to pay a healthy premium for this kind of growth. Instead, you can grab a piece of Nvidia for just 16 times trailing earnings and less than 9 times next year's profit target.

Let's reposition the cameras for a different shot of the same picture. Nvidia stock is down over the past week, even after Monday's historic but short-lived bounce. The shares have plummeted nearly 30% over the past month.

In the past month, we've seen Nvidia's fiscal 2028 per-share profit target rise from $15.91 to $16.61. It's now expected to grow revenue and earnings by more than 70% next year. Last week it delivered its biggest earnings beat in more than a year.

A lot can go wrong. The fast but now vulnerable wheels of the AI trade can get wobbly. Nvidia's record margins can contract. The floor might seem a lot lower than the ceiling may seem higher, but can you honestly feel that way given today's valuation? I believe Nvidia will beat the market from here, making it a rising name on my list of potential stocks to buy in December.

Mamdani handed out $95K in staff raises the same day he warned of $12B budget deficit: report

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani faces intense public backlash, and Staten Island crowd members booed him at a recent event. National correspondent Bryan Llenas reports on independent estimates showing Mamdani's free universal child care program could cost $9.3 billion dollars, fifty-five percent more than claimed.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani approved roughly $850,000 in pay raises for dozens of City Hall aides during his first year in office, according to municipal payroll records reviewed by the New York Post.

The records show the raises were distributed to at least 46 staffers since Mamdani took office in January, bringing total compensation increases near $1 million.

The Post counted 21 new hires who received raises totaling $450,000 before their first anniversary on the job, more than twice the combined number of first-year employees who got raises under Eric Adams and Bill de Blasio.

Mayor Zohran Mamdani speaks to reporters in front of a painting of Alexander Hamilton about the city's finances during a news conference in New York Feb. 17, 2026. (Seth Wenig/AP Photo)

MAMDANI TURNS ON NEW YORK CITY TEACHERS UNION WITH LAWSUIT BLOCKING PAY RAISES FOR EDUCATORS

On Jan. 28, five City Hall employees were granted salary increases totaling $95,000 on the same day Mamdani announced New York City faced an estimated $12 billion budget deficit, the records show. The mayor’s office later lowered that projected deficit to approximately $5.4 billion.

The mayor’s office on Nov. 12 confirmed the city’s fiscal 2027 budget rose $3.3 billion from the budget adopted in June, reaching $129.2 billion.

MAMDANI'S TAXPAYER-FUNDED INFLUENCER ARMY ACCUSED OF PLOTTING PRESSURE CAMPAIGN AGAINST NYC CEOS

The payroll disclosures detail significant salary adjustments across several executive staff positions.

Julia Simcoe and Jacob Russell each received raises of more than $60,000, bringing their salaries to $148,000 and $145,000, respectively, the Post reported. Three other City Hall staffers received raises ranging from $30,000 to $45,000.

Zohran Mamdani, mayor of New York, center, and Julie Menin, speaker of the New York City Council, center right, during an announcement of a handshake agreement on the 2027 budget in the rotunda of City Hall in New York June 30, 2026. (Michael Nagle/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

MAMDANI’S AFFORDABILITY REVOLUTION STUMBLED AS RENT COSTS SOAR TO RECORD HIGHS

The salary increases come as Mamdani, who took office Jan. 1 as the city's first self-described socialist mayor, faces fiscal constraints and administrative hurdles on core campaign pledges.

Mamdani’s plan to eliminate bus fares across the five boroughs has stalled after pushback from New York Gov. Kathy Hochul, who noted the Metropolitan Transportation Authority cannot absorb the loss of revenue from fare boxes. While Mamdani’s campaign estimated the initiative would cost $700 million, the New York City Independent Budget Office (IBO) projected that fully eliminating fares would cost New York State roughly $1.1 billion.

EXPERTS SCORCH MAMDANI'S GROCERY PLAN AS AN 'ILLUSION' THAT WILL HAVE TAXPAYERS FOOTING THE BILL

The administration has also faced capacity limits with its universal childcare initiative. A pilot program aimed at 2-year-olds launched with 2,000 available seats but drew more than 5,700 applicants, leaving roughly 3,700 children on waitlists. The program cost $73 million to launch, and the IBO estimated an expanded citywide version would cost $425 million by 2028.

Meanwhile, Mamdani's plan to establish five city-run grocery stores that sell items 30% below market value has drawn skepticism from retail analysts and economists who question whether the discounts can be sustained without ongoing taxpayer subsidies.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani speaks to reporters during a news conference in New York Feb. 17, 2026. (AP Photo/Seth Wenig)

Fox News Digital asked the mayor's office what civil service guidelines justified the early salary bumps, why raises were authorized the same day Mamdani warned of a $12 billion deficit and how City Hall reconciles the payroll increases with budget constraints on programs like universal childcare and free transit.

A spokesperson for City Hall did not immediately return a request for comment.

Black Friday 2026 live: Best deals, store hours, free giveaways, and real-time updates

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In 2026, Black Friday isn't so much a day as it is a state of mind. Ever since Amazon and Best Buy went live with their official Black Friday sales events on Nov. 19, the entire internet has gone on sale. Is that an exaggeration? Only a little! And now that the leftover turkey is cooling in the fridge and Black Friday is well underway, you can find discounts on virtually every product category.

We're here to help you find the best Black Friday deals on the newest tech, noise-cancelling headphones, Apple products, kitchen appliances, outdoor gear, streaming apps, and the year's most popular gifts. To do that, we're closely monitoring prices at all the major retailers to make sure you get the lowest possible price at any given time. This is where you'll find our most recent Black Friday news, the latest deal drops, low-stock alerts, and important updates for in-person shoppers braving the Black Friday crowds.

For Black Friday and Cyber Monday, our team of shopping reporters and product reviewers works hard to find the deals that are actually worth adding to your cart. We're going to keep a watchful eye out for products with big 50% discounts, record-low prices, and first-time price cuts on new products. After a year of relentless price increases, Black Friday 2026 is your chance to save some money for once. So, when you're not busy with travel, reheating Thanksgiving leftovers, or watching Elf for the hundredth time, join us for some Black Friday retail therapy. You deserve it.

Black Friday 2026 falls on Friday, Nov. 27. However, Black Friday sales have evolved from an in-person shopping bonanza into a weeks-long sales event that takes place online and in stores. As of Nov. 19, all of the major retailers have already started their online Black Friday events.

In 2026, Cyber Monday falls on Monday, Nov. 30. Some online retailers will host a 24-hour sale in honor of the event, but many brands now offer extended sales, typically called Cyber Week sales.

The answer to this question will vary by store and location, but in recent years, most of the big-box retailers have started to close their doors on Thanksgiving or close early, opting to give their employees time off to spend with family and friends. So, while most grocery stores will be open in the morning and early afternoon, many major retailers will be closed, including Best Buy, Target, and Walmart. Thanksgiving is also a bank holiday, so most businesses will be closed or have reduced hours.

No, the day after Thanksgiving, typically known as Black Friday, is not a bank holiday. Even so, many businesses do close on this day, so before visiting a specific store or business, check their holiday hours or call to make sure they're open.

Report links Alice Huang to Chinese firms with smuggled Nvidia chips

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An investigation has linked Alice Huang, the woman at the center of the Nvidia Corp. controversy, to Chinese firms that reportedly possess smuggled chips.

Bloomberg researched the firms in question by reviewing regulatory documents from China. The paper published the results of its analysis today.

In October, the New York Times revealed that Nvidia chief executive Jensen Huang had an affair with Alice Huang. Some U.S. officials reportedly believe she is a spy, while others see her ties to China’s intelligence service as “more circumstantial.” Nvidia’s board ousted Jensen Huang over the affair and reinstated him about two weeks later.

Bloomberg’s investigation focused on the Yangtze River Delta Project, a data center campus in China. The paper found links between the project and Megaspeed, a Singaporean cloud provider that Alice Huang headed until last year. According to the report, Megaspeed at one point described the campus as its largest data center.

The investigation also determined that the Yangtze River Delta Project’s president is a businessman called Kang Wei. Earlier reports identified him as Alice Huang’s husband.

Bloomberg looked into a Chinese company called Shanghai Kezhan that has ties to the Yangtze River Delta Project. The company is reportedly a unit of a Japanese firm that is controlled by Alice Huang. Kezhan stated in a filing that it uses the data center to host 32 servers equipped with Nvidia’s H200 chips, which can’t be legally exported to China.

The company also uses the facility to host a GB200 rack. GB200 systems include 72 of Nvidia’s Blackwell B200 graphics cards, which are significantly more capable than the H200. They too are subject to U.S. export restrictions.

A second Chinese company, Shanghai Shuoyao, has reportedly deployed a GB300 rack at the data center. Such systems are based on the Blackwell Ultra, an upgraded version of the B200 that features significantly more memory. It’s Nvidia’s second fastest graphics card behind its latest Rubin accelerator.

Both Kezhan and Shuoyao described the servers in loan documents. They used the systems as collateral for millions of dollars in debt financing. According to Bloomberg, it’s possible the companies possess additional Nvidia systems but didn’t mention them in the documents because they weren’t used as collateral.

The report raises fresh questions about the effectiveness of Nvidia’s regulatory compliance efforts days before Jensen Huang is scheduled to appear before Congress. According to CNBC, he will testify before three House committees on Sunday and the Senate Banking Committee next Thursday.

The Supernatural Ending Jensen Ackles Actually Wants Is So Much Better Than What We Got

📅 Nov 28, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.
