What Happened To Harrison Ford’s Deckard After Blade Runner 2049? I Asked 2099’s EP For Her Take

Guarding Stars Made A Major Change To The Book's Ending I Don't Understand

I’m In My Feelings Over The Chat Alfonso Ribeiro Had With Harry Shum Jr. And Jenna Johnson After Their DWTS Elimination

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Supernatural may have ended over six years ago, but the series is still a fan-favorite when it comes to rewatches with your Peacock subscription. The show’s 15 seasons were a roller coaster ride involving the Winchester brothers fighting just about everything Hell would throw at them, and more. However, Supernatural’s controversial series finale has given fans a lot to talk about, and a lot of it is not great. After hearing Jensen Ackles’ own version of the ending, I wish we had gotten that instead.

The Supernatural series finale in November 2020 saw Ackles’ Dean getting killed while on a hunt and Sam (Jared Padalecki) living the rest of his life with an unknown wife and a son who was named after his brother. Sam eventually died of old age and met back up with Dean in Heaven. It’s been previously revealed the SPN finale was very different pre-COVID, and a lot of it changed and people had to be cut out, understandably.

Ackles reiterated what the ending would have been had it not been for COVID while at a Supernatural fan convention ( via tvdkami), and I am once again mourning what the pandemic took from us:

Loved to have actually gotten to shoot the ending that we were intending to shoot. That being said, that doesn’t change the outcome of how it ended. So if we got to do the ending that was originally scripted, which I think a lot of you know, and for those that don’t, was Sam comes to Heaven and walks up to The Roadhouse, and I come out, and we have that little meeting that we ended up having on the bridge.

I would have loved more than anything for this to actually happen. The amount of characters that Supernatural introduced and killed off during its run, or just straight-up forgot about, was a large number. He went on to talk about Kansas performing, a moment that would have been absolutely perfect, given that “Carry On Wayward Son” was SPN’s unofficial theme song. It would have looked like this:

But then I say, ‘Hey, come in here. Some people I want you to see.’ We walk through The Roadhouse, it’s all the people from the show, all the characters. The actual band Kansas was going to be the ‘Roadhouse band.’ And then they were signed on to do it. I think one of them was on a plane about to head to Vancouver when they got the call and said, ‘Hey, we’re shutting down. Get off the plane.’

Of course, there is only so much that could have been done at the time with all the restrictions, and at the very least, the series brought back Jim Beaver as Sam and Dean’s late surrogate father, Bobby Singer. That was better than nothing, in my opinion.

There were a lot of expectations for Supernatural's finale, and it's still disappointing to think about, even considering the extenuating circumstances. That hasn't stopped Ackles from sharing his own take about what he would have done with the finale.

For him, he also feels like there was a missed opportunity to end the show on a twist, something Supernatural was known for doing.

So I would have liked for that to happen, but I’ll add just a little extra to that, which is during that kind of reunion, so to speak, I would have liked Jack to come through the front door and just walk up to Sam and Dean and be like, ‘I need to send you guys back.’ And then that’s the end. Then we’re just like, ‘What? Well, wait!’ Which just would have been great, which just opens the door right to something into the future. We can still do it. Not too late. But, by the way, we could go back to that bridge and be just looking out and pull back, and Jack’s behind us, and he’s like, ‘Guys, I hate to do this to you.’

I would have been alright with an ending like this, despite it's shocking nature. Though I'll admit it's mostly because I wasn’t fond of how the finale turned out. Dean’s death dragged on, the penultimate felt more like a finale with how Sam, Dean and Jack defeated God/Chuck, and Castiel dying. Yes, things could and should have been different and they worked with what they had, but I still wish it had played out in a different manner.

Having recently rewatched Supernatural, I saw the finale again, and definitely think it could have been better. However, I’m just glad the series was able to still knock out a finale given the circumstances, and we can still extrapolate out all of this stuff from the cast in hindsight. Could have, should have, would have.

Passionate writer. Obsessed with anything and everything entertainment, specifically movies and television. Can get easily attached to fictional characters.

Azul is a game in which you arrange patterned tiles in order to create the most beautiful tapestry possible. Arranging tiles in a strategic sequence will help you build your score while limiting your opponents’ ability to grow theirs.

Magna-Tiles are a quintessential kids’ toy. The colorful, semi-translucent geometric shapes are a favorite here at The Verge, especially for those of us who prefer the simplicity of building with magnets rather than bricks.

Lego’s 709-piece kit lets you construct three brick-ified bonsai trees, including a black pine, a ginkgo with yellow leaves, and a wisteria with hanging lilac flowers. It also comes with three ornamental displays, just in case you want a raised stand on which to showcase them.

The Beam isn’t as powerful as the Sonos Arc, nor can it match the flagship soundbar’s Dolby Atmos immersion bubble. But you still get room-filling surround sound for less money — and the Beam ties into the Sonos ecosystem for added convenience. Read our review.

Google wants to sell you on the Pro’s software features, but it’s the underlying hardware that makes it a stellar Android handset. The build quality is incredible, the larger telephoto sensor is an improvement, and it now supports faster wired speeds and Qi2.2 for up to 25W wireless charging. Read our review.

Roborock’s first roller-mop robot vacuum, the Curv 2 Flow, features flagship specs at a midrange price, including excellent mopping, a retractable mop cover, 20,000Pa suction, AI-powered obstacle avoidance, and a compact design. Read our review.

The Insta360 Link 2 offers the same auto-swiveling design as its excellent predecessor, only in a smaller form factor. It also offers a slate of new features, including group tracking and no-follow zones.

With 12.4mm drivers, active noise cancellation, and an IP54 rating, the CMF Buds 2A offer high-end features at an affordable price. You can also customize the sound, adjust ANC levels, and find the earbuds using the Nothing X app.

These smart lights have beautiful colors, a nice selection of festive scenes, and an easy-to-use app. They are super reliable when paired with a Hue Bridge and also work over Bluetooth. They come in three lengths, work with all the major smart home platforms, and are Matter-compatible. Read our review.

The Echo Dot Max features a flat design and a redesigned sound system, which delivers nearly three times the bass as its predecessor. It also offers improved conversation detection and better noise filtering, thanks to Amazon’s new AZ3 chip. Read our review.

Glocusent’s clip-in reading light features five brightness levels and multiple color temperature settings, ranging from a soft, warm glow that’s great for late-night reading to a brighter light that’s perfect for daytime use. The slim USB light easily slips into your pocket, too, so you can read anywhere, from a cozy bed to a dimly lit train.

Vantage isn’t your typical open-world exploration game. It’s a sci-fi roguelike that has you traversing an alien planet alongside up to five players, each of whom must solve puzzles and complete their mission before their time, morale, or health runs out. It’s not as charming as Stonemaier’s award-winning Wingspan, but it is far more interesting.

A quiet, battery-powered fan that moves a lot of air despite its small size, the Switchbot Standing Circulator is a surprisingly versatile smart home addition.

The last-gen JBL Go 4 has been around for a bit, but the pocket-sized Bluetooth speaker still delivers with up to seven hours of runtime, dual-device connectivity, and an IP67 rating for water and dust resistance. You can also pair it with other Go 4 speakers for bigger stereo sound, if desired.

Fairphone’s latest is a very light update to the 6, adding a newer processor and carrying forward its repairability and ethical values. Tradeoffs compared to a traditional smartphone are few, and the benefits are real.

The proliferation of miniature screens continues with Ugreen’s latest Nexode USB-C Cable, which features a full-color LCD screen on one end that displays current power draw (up to 240W). It also supports 480Mbps data transfer speeds and, unlike similar power meter-equipped cables, lets you turn off the screen when needed.

The Apple Watch SE 3 is a major glow-up, adding an always-on display, wrist-temperature sensor, a more powerful processor, and fast charging. Read our review.

Nintendo’s latest Pokémon spinoff for the Switch 2 is part Pokémon, part Minecraft, and part Animal Crossing. Yet, despite borrowing gameplay mechanics from each, the cozy life sim is more than the sum of its expansive, building-centric parts. It’s breezy and warm-hearted, with enough secrets to keep you occupied for hours on end.

Google’s new Pixel 10A introduces faster wired charging, Satellite SOS, and new AI tools like Camera Coach and Auto Best Take. Otherwise, it’s basically the Pixel 9A, with the same Tensor G4 chip and a dual-camera system. Read our review.

The entry point into RTX Spark laptops, the Asus ProArt is a creator-focused MacBook Pro wannabe with a sleek blacked-out design and lovely OLED display. Read our initial Nvidia RTX Spark hands-on.

There are many gaming headsets out there, but few as elegant as Fractal’s Scape. The PC-focused (but Switch- and PlayStation-compatible) wireless headset offers great sound and includes a wireless charger, and it has easy-to-master physical controls.

Everyone knows gift cards aren’t the best gift, but with such steep discounts happening on Nintendo eShop credit, you can save a lot on a Switch 2, or games and accessories, if you’re alright with jumping through a couple extra hoops.

USB-C display glasses can make travel (or relaxing on the couch) more fun by putting a huge virtual screen in front of your face. Xreal’s One Pro are some of the best out there at displaying content from your phone, tablet, laptop, or portable handheld.

Govee’s Permanent Outdoor Lights 2 are Matter-ready smart lights you can leave up year-round. They can display multiple colors at once, with 100 preset lighting scenes, customizable effects, and the ability to sync to music. The lights also feature an IP67 rating for dust and water resistance.

The Ecobee Smart Thermostat Premium is an excellent option for adding smarts and some style to your HVAC system. As well as being a smart way to control your energy use, the thermostat can also act as a video intercom for Ecobee’s excellent doorbell camera and an indoor air quality monitor. Read our review.

The Cricut Joy 2 is a small crafting machine designed for smaller projects, including custom cards, stickers, vinyl decals, labels, bookmarks, and iron-ons. It can cut designs up to 4.5 inches wide and brings Cricut’s Print Then Cut feature to its entry-level lineup. Read our review.

Although it’s not Amazon’s first bedside alarm clock, the Echo Time is focused on helping you sleep better. The camera-less Alexa speaker can detect your sleep stage via ultrasound and use smart alarms to ease you into the day, but only if you want it to.

The90’s Gem is a UV-tracking necklace that measures UVA and UVB exposure throughout the day. Its subscription-free companion app tracks exposure trends, offers sunscreen reminders, and uses an optional skin profile to estimate sunburn risk and vitamin D production from sunlight. Read our hands-on impressions.

Power over Ethernet provides this 2K resolution video doorbell with rock-solid connectivity while reducing the number of wires you need. There’s no subscription required to view recorded video: it can record locally on a microSD card, Reolink Hub, or FTP server and connect to a platform like Home Assistant.

The Pro 3 features swappable ABXY face buttons and joystick caps, providing players with a highly customizable experience. It also features new L4 and R4 macro buttons between the triggers and its USB-C charging port, along with more durable and power efficient TMR joysticks.

Camp Snap’s latest digital point-and-shoot camera may still lack a screen and capture only 8-megapixel stills, but it offers a slimmer design, faster performance, and filters right out of the box. That, along with several other new features, makes it easier for kids to use.

AI Audit Evidence, Part 9: One "Not Executed" Can Stand for Two Pasts

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After an outbound write times out, the system asks the target one read-only question: did you execute this key? The target answers "not executed". The ledger takes that as "nothing landed", releases the hold, and lets the call be made again.

But that answer can stand for two different pasts, and they point to opposite decisions.

This piece is about why a true answer can still be ambiguous, what we now record alongside it, and why that extra fact has to stay a label rather than a gate.

B: it was accepted and executed, and the record was later reversed or deleted, so it cannot be found now. For the retry question, A is safe to retry. B is a second execution of a write that already happened.

Both look the same from the target: "not found". The difference is not in whether the answer is true — it is — but in what the answer was read from: the target's live object store, or its own acceptance record.

The probe has one mode: a single GET against the status path the tool declares. A non-GET method, an absolute path, or an unfilled placeholder is treated as a configuration error and no request is sent at all.

So the answer is always "is it there now", never "was it ever there".

Worse: the path says nothing about the source. Both kinds of past are asked with the same single GET, so the method, the path and the response shape cannot tell them apart. Any rule that guesses the kind from which field the path points at is claiming something the target never said.

And one more layer: if the target's read path is eventually consistent, a very recent acceptance may not have reached it yet. Then "not there now" does not even equal "not there now, truly".

We made the distinction a field — a basis:

current_state: the target answered from its live object store.

receipt: the target answered from its own acceptance or event record.

absent: the target did not say, or the row predates the field. Three choices are worth spelling out.

First: if the target does not say, we record "unknown". We do not infer it from the method or the path. That is not caution for its own sake — it is the same rule this series keeps coming back to: do not let an empty value speak for the system.

Second: a receipt is not an authority claim. An eventually consistent receipt can still miss a recent acceptance. The field says what the answer is based on, not how true it is. It is a label, not a confidence score — and if that is not said plainly, a reader will treat it as a second conclusion.

Third: the column is nullable, with no default and no backfill. A default would be easier, and it would stamp every historical row as "current state", destroying the reading "this row predates the field". That would be one more case of speaking for the data.

"Not executed" releases the hold and allows a retry regardless of kind.

Why not a gate: a target with no status endpoint would then never be releasable. Translating "the evidence is weak" into "you may not retry" leaves some systems with nowhere to go.

If the basis is ever wired into the retry decision, it may only raise the bar — a weak reading can require a recorded decision, but it cannot block automatically. That boundary is written into our own remediation list.

A release is a decision. The row already carries the readings themselves: how many asks, and the latest three — time, conclusion, cause, who asked, and now the basis. A reader then asked: should the grade be stored on the decision row too?

A reading is an event — append-only, never rewritten — so copying it cannot drift. A grade is a judgement computed from readings; it changes when the rule changes, and two copies guarantee one of them is stale. A reader put the rule better than I had: an event may be copied; a judgement may not.

Everything above is what we have. Two gaps remain, listed because they come from the same rule.

Readable history: zero. Today no target can be asked for history through the probe. Reading history is a new mode, not a bigger query. So we can say "this reading is a current-state answer"; we cannot yet say "this is what it was before".

The decision row cannot resolve back to the reading row. The copied entries still carry no reading id, so they cannot be followed to their source. Scheduled ahead of the rest. One more case of the same rule has already been closed: when the target has no status endpoint at all, it cannot even be asked — and the release row used to say the same sentence for "cannot ask" as for "nobody asked". It now reads "not askable — this tool has no usable status query", and the same fact goes into the evidence column as a field.

None of this changes the argument; it only shows that the same rule is still owed elsewhere.

A true sentence can still be a present tense with more than one past.

"What kind of answer is this" is not a score; it is a question about where the answer came from. Sooner or later the target has to answer it once. Until then, the honest move is to record that we do not know the source — not to pick one on its behalf.

Building a multi-tenant real-estate CRM for Indian developers: 5 decisions we'd make again

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We build Sthan, a CRM for Indian real-estate developers. It handles leads from property portals, ads and WhatsApp, site visits, tower and unit inventory, bookings, construction-linked payment plans and possession. One codebase serves every builder (tenant). These are the decisions that held up.

A ₹1.47 crore flat is 1470000000 paise. No floats, no decimals in transit, and formatting happens only at the edge. Cost sheets, GST lines and demand notes add up to the paisa, and every rounding rule lives in one module with tests.

The tenant id comes from the session, never from the request body. Raw SQL is treated as a privileged act: each raw query is listed, reviewed and scanned in CI, so a cross-tenant path can't slip in quietly.

Add a column, backfill, switch reads, then (much later) drop. Staging gets every change first; production only moves a commit that was green on staging.

Buyer phone numbers and ID-like fields are masked in list responses and logs. The full value is fetched only for the one action that needs it.

A unit "held" for a buyer carries an expiry, so two sales executives can't sell the same flat, and a forgotten hold returns the unit to the market on its own.

Stack: NestJS + Prisma + PostgreSQL on the API, React + Vite on the web, React Native (Expo) for the sales-team app.

What would you add to this list? Questions welcome in the comments.

Acceptance criteria your AI coding agent can actually test

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Most acceptance criteria I see in PRDs are wishes, not tests. "The dashboard should load quickly." "Users should be able to reset their password easily." "The search should work well."

Hand those to an AI coding agent and it will nod, build something, and call the feature done — because there is nothing in the sentence it can fail against. A criterion the agent cannot test is a criterion the agent cannot satisfy. It is decoration.

Good acceptance criteria are written so that a machine — or a tired reviewer at 11pm — can check them and say yes or no without guessing. Here is how I write them.

The format is not the point. The values are.

The strong version names the data, the route, the number, the order, and the time. An agent can build a test for every clause. The weak version has no clause at all — "see their tasks" could mean anything, and the agent will pick the easiest interpretation.

Rule: if a criterion has no number, no named state, and no named outcome, it is not finished yet.

A feature is only defined when you also say what happens when it goes wrong. For every success case, write the failure case next to it:

Agents are optimistic builders. Left alone, they build the happy path and leave the error state as a browser alert or a silent nothing. The failure twin is what forces the error UI, the validation, and the "what happens next" into the build — before a real user discovers the gap.

The bugs that cost you a weekend are never in the main flow. They are in the edges: empty lists, duplicate submissions, expired sessions, zero results, amounts of exactly zero.

Write the edge case as a criterion with the same GIVEN / WHEN / THEN shape. If it lives in a footnote ("handle empty states"), the agent will not build it. If it is a numbered criterion, it becomes a test the agent runs before calling the feature done.

"The API should use a database index" is not an acceptance criterion. It is an implementation detail wearing a criterion's clothes, and it cannot be observed from the outside.

The agent is free to add the index, cache the query, or paginate — whatever satisfies the observable number. Criteria that prescribe the implementation tie the agent's hands and still fail to say what the user experiences. Criteria that state the observable outcome let the agent choose the means and let you verify the end.

A criterion that says three things is a criterion that passes when two of them work:

Three checks, each pass or fail on its own. When the agent runs them, a failure points at exactly one broken thing instead of a vague "signup doesn't work".

In the four files I generate, acceptance criteria sit in PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md — one GIVEN / WHEN / THEN block per feature, success and failure side by side, edge cases as numbered criteria. AI_AGENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md then tells the agent to treat each criterion as a check: build the feature, run the checks, do not call it done until every one passes. TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md turns the numbers into indexes, validation schemas, and idempotency keys, and UI_UX_SPECIFICATION.md carries the empty states and error states the failure cases demand.

The point is not the format. The point is that the agent has something to test against — and that you have something to hold it to when it says "done".

Read any criterion in your PRD and ask: could I write a test for this without asking the author a question? If the answer is no — no number, no named state, no observable outcome — rewrite it before the build starts. It takes five minutes per feature, and it is the difference between an agent that finishes your feature and an agent that finishes something that looks like your feature.

If you want the same starting point, the generator writes GIVEN / WHEN / THEN criteria into every feature it produces — you can try it free with 50 credits at webdevmehedi.com/ai-prd-generator — and the free template behind the format is in the ai-prd-template repo.

A PRD without testable criteria is a wish list. Write the test into the wish, and the agent will build what you actually asked for.

Web Dev Mehedi turns an app idea into a build-ready PRD, UI/UX spec, database schema, API contracts, and Cursor/Claude Code rules in under 60 seconds. Free signup includes 50 credits; paid plans start at ৳99/month.

P3dPyPad: F5 Hot-Reload Python Workbench & WASM 3D Studio for AutoCAD Plant 3D

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Developing parametric Python components for AutoCAD Plant 3D has a major productivity bottleneck: because of Python module caching inside the AutoCAD process, modifying a script normally forces you to restart AutoCAD (often 30–60 seconds per iteration).

I built and open-sourced P3dPyPad (GPL-3.0) to fix this workflow and add offline simulation + batch testing.

Try out the browser WASM studio or check out the component scripts on GitHub!

SBI brings Japanese stocks to Solana but investors face a five-day wait to cash out

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SBI’s Solana expansion brings Japanese equities to more investors through USDC, with redemptions taking five dealing days.

SBI Solana Global began full-scale operations on Nov. 25, with plans to bring Japanese financial assets to more markets.

Eligible professional investors can use USDC to buy exposure to a Japanese dividend stock fund through Hong Kong’s HashKey Earn. To exit, they submit a redemption request, and HashKey expects settlement on T+5 under the issuer’s dealing calendar and settlement arrangements.

SBI Holdings, Sumitomo Mitsui Financial Group and the Solana Foundation said they completed agreements for a new joint-venture structure and that the former SBI R3 Japan began operating under its new name.

The partners plan to use Solana to distribute Japanese assets and develop services in stablecoin payments, financial and real-world asset tokenization, and on-chain asset management.

The partners intend to start with practical uses in Japan before expanding across Asia and linking up with global markets.

DigiFT and SBI Global Asset Management announced the token JX on July 15. DigiFT issued the token on Solana as a depositary receipt linked to the SBI Japan High Dividend Equity Fund’s growth type, managed by SBI Asset Management.

The fund invests mainly in listed Japanese companies and seeks returns from dividends and rising share prices. DigiFT says the growth structure makes no distributions at either the fund or token level.

Qualified investors first gained access through Singapore-based DigiFT on July 14, according to SBI Global Asset Management. On Nov. 5, SBI Global Asset Management announced a second distribution channel through HashKey Earn, a service of the licensed Hong Kong platform HashKey Exchange.

That gave eligible investors a second place to buy JX while SBI Asset Management continued to run the underlying portfolio.

Clients must qualify as professional investors under HashKey and issuer rules and complete applicable onboarding and suitability checks.

The public dealing terms list a minimum subscription of 5,000 USDC and a minimum redemption of 750,000 JX tokens, and the platform lists a 0.1% redemption fee.

The daily cutoff is 9 a.m. Hong Kong time. HashKey expects settlement in five dealing days for subscriptions and redemptions after the applicable dealing date.

The final price and timing depend on the product’s valuation and settlement arrangements. A non-dealing day in Japan, Singapore, or the US can affect processing or settlement.

HashKey does not support external JX wallet deposits or withdrawals, transfers between its users, or secondary trading in JX. An investor who holds the token through HashKey Earn has to submit a redemption request there to exit.

Payouts are in USDC, while the underlying stocks are mostly denominated in yen, so exchange-rate movements can still affect returns.

In an Oct. 6 statement on Project Trinity, the participants said SBI Securities and Daiwa Securities settled two test transfers using a privately placed security-token bond from SBI VC Trade and a trust-based stablecoin from Sumitomo Mitsui Banking Corporation.

Both instruments were issued for the trial, and the test paired delivery of the bond token with payment in the stablecoin.

A JX investor on HashKey Earn goes through a fund redemption instead. The investor receives USDC after the request clears the product’s valuation and dealing process.

Senate Democrats urge SEC to scrap crypto, tokenized stock exemptions

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

US Senators Elizabeth Warren and Chris Van Hollen, among others, have taken aim at two recent SEC efforts to ease regulatory requirements for the crypto industry.

US Senators Elizabeth Warren and Chris Van Hollen have urged the US Securities and Exchange Commission to drop a proposal that would allow eligible crypto projects to raise funds from US investors without registering their offerings and revoke an exemption for tokenized stock platforms.

In two letters sent to SEC Chair Paul Atkins on Tuesday, the Democratic lawmakers argued that the agency had exceeded its statutory authority in both cases and that the moves will put investors at risk.

“Together, the letters raise concerns about the SEC’s efforts to carve out special treatment for crypto assets and tokenized securities at the expense of investor protections and market integrity,” they said.

Both SEC measures drew crypto industry support, as they came at a time when the CLARITY Act struggled to advance in Congress. The Regulation Crypto Assets was first introduced in August when the landmark crypto legislation was stalled, while the tokenized stock exemption came out in September, two days after the CLARITY Act fell short of the 60 votes needed to advance.

The letters also come just a day after OKXICE, a joint venture between crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange, launched a round-the-clock trading venue for tokenized US securities under the exemption on Monday.

The tokenized stock exemption allows qualifying platforms to offer blockchain-based trading of tokenized US stocks without registering as exchanges.

SEC lists four other entities that intend to use the exemption, including TSV LLC (which commenced operation in early November), Kephas Corporation d/b/a Perpetuals.com, Formation Securities LLC and Fairmint TSV LLC.

Van Hollen, Warren and two other senators urged the SEC to withdraw the order, warning the order could create a “shadow stock market” that falls outside of regulatory protections.

“We are particularly troubled by the order’s sweeping exemption of TSVs from fundamental registration requirements,” they said.

They also argued the order places an “unreasonable burden” on publicly traded companies by allowing third parties to tokenize their securities unless the issuer objects within 30 days.

“This effectively establishes an opt-out regime in which companies must continuously monitor tokenization activity and respond to potentially numerous requests from different platforms to prevent unauthorized trading of tokenized versions of their shares.”

Related: Trump’s SEC nominee Johnson backs formal rulemaking for tokenized stock trading

The senators also argued venues are exempt from rules requiring them to display their best prices to traders. “These protections are foundational to the fairness and efficiency of our equity markets,” they said.

Atkins previously described the five-year exemption as an interim measure to allow onchain trading of certain US stocks while the agency considers longer-term rules.

In a separate letter, Warren, Van Hollen and Senator Tina Smith urged the SEC to withdraw its Regulation Crypto Assets Proposal, arguing that the proposal only serves to help crypto assets “escape” from the SEC’s regulatory regime.

The proposal would allow eligible crypto projects to raise up to $5 million over four years under a startup exemption or up to $75 million in a 12-month period under a separate fundraising exemption, without registering the offerings with the SEC.

The senators argued these exemptions would let crypto issuers raise money without prior SEC review, particularly the $5 million startup exemption, which would allow early-stage projects to raise money without providing financial statements.

“The proposal rationalizes the startup exemption with the idea that these offerings are from startups, and its only limitation is a $5 million offering cap. However, the exemption does nothing to actually protect investors – it simply means that the SEC is willing for investors to be defrauded in $5 million increments,” they said.

The SEC has previously said that the exemptions would require issuers to give investors written disclosures, and federal anti-fraud and anti-manipulation rules would still apply.

Cointelegraph reached out to the SEC for comment.

Magazine: Can the SEC and CFTC’s pro-crypto rules be overturned? Legal opinion

OpenAI Isn’t Planning Prompt-Level Reports For ChatGPT Ads via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: State Of Agentic AEO For 2027: What’s Scaling & What’s Stalling

ChatGPT ads match to queries OpenAI's own system writes. Advertisers can't see them, and OpenAI says prompt-level reports aren't planned. Here's what it means.

OpenAI doesn’t plan to provide ChatGPT advertisers with reports showing the prompts or inferred intent behind their ads. When Adweek asked whether advertisers could expect such reports, Vijaye Raji, OpenAI’s CTO of applications, said they should not expect them.

Three executives who run OpenAI’s ad system told Adweek that ChatGPT ads are matched to search queries written by the company’s own AI agent. Advertisers don’t see those queries, only aggregate results.

OpenAI’s basics page for advertisers explains that Ads Manager Beta reporting currently includes impressions, clicks, spend, CTR, average CPC, average CPM and conversions.

OpenAI’s “About this ad” panel tells users why they saw an ad and what performance data advertisers receive.

OpenAI’s ad system begins by creating one or more search queries based upon buying intent determined from the conversation, per the company’s ads engineering post published Oct. 21 by Ritendra Datta and Karthik Gopalratnam. OpenAI calls the model that creates these search queries the orchestrator. Using these search queries, the ad system retrieves candidate ads, filters them, scores them and identifies the top candidates to be entered into an auction to set the price and select the winner. Returning no ad is called “an ordinary, successful outcome” in the post.

As described in the post, if a user asks about a two-day Yosemite trip and then about a shoe brand’s new product lines, the ad system may create a query representing that conversation such as “shoes appropriate for hiking trip,” even though the user did not connect the two questions.

According to the post, when ads personalization is turned on, the orchestrator will also consider “nonsensitive information about the user’s past conversations.”

ChatGPT users in the European Economic Area and Switzerland will not have access to personalized ads at first, according to OpenAI’s ads FAQ for users. Brooke Osmundson covered the engineering post in October.

Advertisers share context hints about where an ad might fit, and OpenAI says these hints help guide matching without determining it.

“Hints are not targeting rules or delivery instructions. They do not guarantee that your ad will appear for particular conversations, audiences, or situations, and they cannot enforce geographic limits, schedules, or exclusions.”

The basics page also says that hints “are not exact-match keywords.” Negative Phrases help qualifying advertisers limit where their ads appear, for “narrow placement requirements specific to their brand’s own policies.” The option is not necessarily available to every advertiser.

Google explains that for some AI searches, the search terms provided in Google Ads reporting reflect the user’s intent rather than the exact query. According to Google’s ad group prioritization page, for searches done through Lens, AI Mode, AI Overviews and autocomplete, “the search term shown in your reporting represents the best approximation of the user’s intent.” Brooke posted about Google’s update to that page in May.

OpenAI does not list a search terms report on its basics page.

ChatGPT advertisers can determine what a campaign delivered, but they cannot see the conversations or queries that led to the results. Click-based and conversion-based campaigns can use the conversion tracking OpenAI supports through Ads Manager Beta. The basics page also describes how UTM parameters added to landing page URLs persist on ad clicks.

OpenAI recommends writing your context hints in terms of customer needs and scenarios instead of listing keywords. Also, use OpenAI’s product controls where available to restrict delivery by geography.

OpenAI says in its ads FAQ for users that it “may explore additional measurement insights over time while continuing to protect user privacy.” OpenAI sends new feature announcements by email to account holders. Check the reporting list on the basics page for updates.

RoboSense Reports Third Quarter 2026 Unaudited Financial Results, with Robotics LiDAR Sales Volume Ranking No. 1 Globally

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HONG KONG, Nov. 25, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- RoboSense Technology Co., Ltd ("RoboSense" or the "Company") (HKEX: 02498), a pioneering robotics technology platform company for Physical AI, today announced its unaudited financial results for the nine months ended September 30, 2026.

Space Camera Enters Supply Chains of Leading Global AI Companies

RoboSense's Space Camera has been showcased at major robotics conferences, including ICRA, IROS and CoRL, gaining widespread recognition across academia and industry. The Company has entered the supply chains of several leading global AI companies and established collaborations with leading AI model developers in North America and several leading humanoid robotics companies worldwide. Its products have been granted testing validation certificates by several leading customers, paving the way for future deployment at scale.

Space Camera is becoming a gateway for robots to acquire real-world spatial data, serving both data collection and humanoid robot applications. For data collection, RoboSense is working with AI model companies and data collection partners to validate egocentric data capture and motion capture. For humanoid robots, Space Camera serves as their eyes, supporting mobility and dexterous manipulation. Consistent perception across both applications helps bridge the gap between model training and real-world deployment.

Robotics LiDAR Growth and Customer Expansion

Sales volume of LiDAR products for robotics and others reached approximately 427,800 units in the first nine months of 2026, up about 423.0% year over year. RoboSense continues to expand its digital LiDAR applications across commercial cleaning, autonomous delivery, embodied robotics and 3D spatial data collection.

As robots move from demonstrations to real-world applications, RoboSense is focused on improving product reliability and integration efficiency, while leveraging its existing robotics customer base to support the commercialization of new products. The integration of E2 and EM4 with NVIDIA Holoscan Sensor Bridge further expands its robotics development ecosystem.

LiDAR sales volume for ADAS applications reached approximately 866,900 units in the first nine months, up about 133.9% year over year. As of September 30, RoboSense had secured design wins for 197 vehicle models with 36 automotive OEMs and Tier 1 suppliers, with 91 models having achieved SOP. The company aims to expand digital LiDAR adoption into vehicles in mainstream price segments and increase LiDAR content value per vehicle through a combination of main LiDAR and blind-spot LiDAR.

This strategy is supported by advancements in RoboSense's in-house semiconductor chips and manufacturing capabilities. The first vehicle model equipped with LiDAR powered by RoboSense's Phoenix SPAD-SoC chip entered mass production and began deliveries in November 2026. In the same month, RoboSense commissioned the world's first fully automated digital LiDAR production line in Shenzhen. The line has an overall automation rate of 99% and an annual production capacity of 500,000 units, and requires only six operators at the final assembly stage.

RoboSense (HKEX: 02498) is a pioneering robotics technology platform company for Physical AI. Founded in 2014 and headquartered in Shenzhen, China, the company has offices in Shanghai, Suzhou, Hong Kong, Munich, Detroit, Silicon Valley, and other locations.

Built on its in-house optoelectronic chips, perception algorithms, and automotive-grade mass production capabilities, RoboSense provides core robotics components, including digital LiDAR and RGB-D Space Cameras.

Its digital LiDAR products have been deployed across more than ten application areas, including intelligent driving, autonomous delivery, robotic lawn mowing, and embodied intelligence. The company ranks No. 1 globally in robotics LiDAR market share.

Looking ahead, through the scaled deployment of Space Camera, digital LiDAR and other products, RoboSense will expand the use of its Space Cameras and digital LiDAR to collect data for robot learning and provide onboard sensing, building the perception foundation for Physical AI.

US announces new tariffs on host of countries including China

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Watch: What we know about Trump's Thanksgiving tariffs

The US has announced a fresh raft of tariffs as President Donald Trump pushes ahead with his signature trade policy.

The new taxes charged on imports to America are set to hit many of the country's biggest trading partners - China, the European Union and 12 other jurisdictions, including Japan, South Korea and Taiwan.

The White House began investigating the trade practices of other countries in March after the US Supreme Court ruled Trump's previously announced Liberation Day tariffs were illegal.

China is facing the highest tariff rate of up to 40%, followed by Vietnam at up to 35%, with others facing either 20% or 25%.

The US Trade Representative (USTR) announced the proposed tariffs together with extensive reports of how each jurisdiction's trade practices purportedly impact American commerce.

The new duties are not expected to take effect until next year and have to go through rounds of public consultation and, potentially, hearings.

Tariffs are taxes levied on imported goods. The amount is typically calculated as a percentage of a good's value and paid to the government by businesses importing products.

Some of these rates will be effectively reduced because of caps on tariffs Trump has agreed to. Individual countries also face different levies.

The proposed tariffs will affect goods from China, the European Union, Singapore, Switzerland, Indonesia, Malaysia, Cambodia, Thailand, Korea, Vietnam, Taiwan, Bangladesh, Japan, and India.

The USTR's Jamieson Greer said the latest tariffs issued would "level the playing field for American workers and chart a new, more sustainable course for the world economy".

He said that many of America's trading partners had "for decades" adopted industrial strategies which included "targeting manufacturing, wage and labor suppression".

They also employ "other market distorting practices that tilt the trade balance in their favour while eroding America's manufacturing base", Greer said.

But economists have warned tariffs can fuel inflation and push prices up for consumers if businesses pass on higher costs, as well as dampen global trade.

Trump has repeatedly threatened or imposed tariffs during his second term, arguing they will boost US manufacturing and create jobs.

Despite a poor result for the Republican party in the midterm elections, the president has continued to press on with his flagship trade policy.

Trump is also seeking to cut the US trade deficit - the gap between the value of goods it buys from other countries and those it sells to them.

Trump's so-called Liberation Day tariffs were struck down by the Supreme Court earlier this year

These new levies are build on a "stronger legal foundation" under US law compared with Trump's April 2025 Liberation Day tariffs, Singapore Management University economics lecturer Chang Pao-Li said.

Trump previously invoked emergency presidential powers to impose tariffs that have since been struck down, while his latest approach relies on the USTR's authority to impose tariffs to address certain foreign trade practices, she said.

The levies are still likely to face legal challenges, but there has been a "historical precedent" for such tariffs remaining in force, Chang said.

Many tariffs imposed under Section 301 - a law that allows the US to probe and retaliate against foreign trade practices - during Trump's first term remained in effect during his successor Joe Biden's administration.

However, Stephen Brown, chief North America economist for Capital Economics, said the latest tariff announcement was "not as alarming as it looks", due to the taxes "essentially replacing" previously announced tariffs made in the summer.

"The increase in the effective tariff rate looks set to be a touch lower than we've been assuming for our forecasts, although the new tariffs will still provide a marginal boost to prices," he said in a note.

The additional tariff burden is likely to be small because a limited number of goods are subject to the new duties, Brown added.

Jurisdictions affected by the new wave of tariffs have voiced their disapproval over the Trump administration's move.

Washington has also proposed a 10% tariff on goods from Brazil, as part of a separate investigation over what it says are "harmful trade practices" undercutting American workers.

Brazilian authorities expressed their "outrage" at the announcement, saying that the move is aimed at pressuring the government to change regulations around issues like digital trade.

The South American nation faces duties on finished aircraft and sugar sold to the US.

"Brazil will not yield to such threats," President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva's office said.

The European Commission rejected the new US tariffs, saying it would investigate the reasons for bringing them in. It pointed out that tariffs are limited to the 15% previously agreed.

The latest tariff rate is "not consistent" with the reality of bilateral economic-trade cooperation between the US and Vietnam, Vietnamese Deputy Prime Minister Bui Thanh Son said.

The Japanese government said the US tariffs were "regrettable", while the Singapore government said it disagrees with the White House's findings and that it is engaging the USTR to explore ways to reduce the tariff.

South Korea's trade minister said the proposed 15% tariff will be "more than a decent outcome" as it would fall in line with a previously agreed limit with Washington.

Last year, Seoul pledged $350bn (Â£267bn) in US investments in exchange for lower tariffs as part of a trade deal.

Trump tariffs on Asia: Which economies were hit hardest?

China's growth accelerates as AI boom helps boost exports

US trading partners have been "waiting for this shoe to drop" and have argued that they are being wrongly accused of producing too much, said the Asia Society Policy Institute's Wendy Cutler, a former senior US trade negotiator.

"Many believe that China, not them, should be the target for US action," she added.

Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesperson Guo Jiakun described the new tariffs as a form of "political manipulation", describing as "false" the US claim that China produces more than it can consume.

The tariff announcement is likely to increase tensions between the US and China, the world's two biggest economies, who remain locked in a trade dispute and the AI race.

The two countries have also been embroiled in a tit-for-tat tariffs war, which is currently on hold.

Despite last week's meeting between Trump and China's President Xi Jinping, the two sides made no announcement on the trade war truce, which is set to expire on 10 January.

Beijing has remained vague on how it will respond but for now seems "prepared to live with the new rates provided they don't exceed 20%", Cutler said.

Additional reporting by Leehyun Choi in Seoul

AXIS: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AI-powered underwriting cuts quote times by 70%, letting the company scale without bloating fixed costs.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 11:10PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Axis Capital ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 29, 2026, AXIS (AXS +1.14%) announced gross written premiums rose 11% year over year to $2.4 billion. For long-term shareholders, disciplined risk selection protects underwriting margins while broader insurance pricing moderates. Expanding book value per share, the core measure of net asset growth, creates compounding potential without pushing into mispriced coverage. Three things management said stand out.

When insurance rates drop below loss costs in broad liability and property lines, writing more coverage destroys value. AXIS cut liability and cyber premiums by 8% and 14%, respectively, year to date -- primarily in reinsurance -- to shield underwriting profits, choosing to expand instead in higher-return niches like marine war risk.

Automation in specialized underwriting frees up human capacity while speeding up business execution. The company rolled out a next-generation underwriting platform that reduced quote cycle times by up to 70% for lower-middle-market accounts, accelerating growth without adding fixed overhead.

Buying back stock below intrinsic value boosts per-share net asset value faster than unneeded premium growth. Management retired 16.6% of common shares since late 2023, expanding repurchases as market pricing softened across property and casualty lines.

Rising portfolio yields drive fixed income returns higher, providing steady cash flow as primary market rates moderate. Specialty underwriting discipline and selective capital deployment maintain portfolio margins without chasing market volume.

Targeted exposure management ensures long-term compounding across shifting risk cycles.

Margin pressure from memory chip costs and a consumer slump sits alongside a $1 billion savings target that is still ahead.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 11:10PM EST

On its fourth-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 24, 2026, HP (HPQ +11.49%) announced fourth-quarter revenue of $16 billion, representing 9% growth year over year. Commercial hardware demand and the adoption of artificial intelligence helped offset consumer market softness and rising component costs. HP returned over $2.3 billion to shareholders through dividends and share repurchases in fiscal year 2026. Three takeaways from the call stand out for long-term investors.

Enterprise clients are accelerating device upgrades as local artificial intelligence (AI) processing reduces latency and keeps proprietary corporate data on-site. Demand for higher-value commercial devices and workstations lifted Personal Systems segment revenue 14% year over year.

Escalating memory chip costs and excess channel inventory pulled consumer revenue down 7% year over year, compressing Personal Systems operating margins to 3%. Management is reducing bill-of-materials costs and adjusting pricing to restore segment margins to target levels before fiscal year-end.

Declining office printing supplies volume reduced overall print segment revenue 1% year over year, but expansion in subscription services and refillable tank printers protected segment profitability. Refillable tank hardware placements increased total customer lifetime value and lifted the printing segment's operating margins above target levels.

Component inflation and soft personal computer demand create near-term friction, but structural cost reductions and expanding enterprise adoption of edge computing create a clearer path to profit recovery. Management relies on disciplined pricing, software attach, and commercial workstation growth while modernizing global supply chains.

Free cash flow generation supports consistent shareholder returns, including an 11th consecutive annual dividend increase. Enterprise customers migrating artificial intelligence tasks locally provide HP a durable structural tailwind.

‘South Park’ Season 29 Finale: ‘GTA 6’ Inspires Stan, Kyle and Cartman to Get to the Bottom of South America’s Power Outages

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the sixth and final episode of “South Park” Season 29, South America has once again been hit with major power outages — but this time it’s serious, considering “Grand Theft Auto VI” has finally been released, which means the residents of the mountain town will stop at nothing to restore their Xboxes and PlayStations and continue playing the long-awaited video game.

“Damn it, Mayor! We have waited over a decade for this game to come out,” proclaims one angry South American resident at the top of the episode. “Do you have any idea how ‘Grand Theft Auto VI’ works? If it shuts up while it’s saving or during a mission, you lose everything.” The issue, though, is that the South American government has made a deal with a group of billionaire weenies, as was introduced in the season’s second episode, who are using the town’s energy to power their data centers. Related Stories Why Denis Villeneuve Cast Robert Pattinson as James Bond: 'I Need to Believe This Man Is a Killer' Elizabeth Debicki Says 'Tenet' Co-Star Robert Pattinson Will Be the ‘Perfect’ James Bond: ‘I’m So Thrilled’

The town’s politicians have no idea what to do about the issue, but luckily for them, a group of angry townspeople barge into South American City Hall and proclaim they know who’s behind the outages: the Chinese. More specifically, the outages are the result of Chinese cyberattacks. Relieved, the town’s politicians let them believe this is the case — “Yes, you figured it out. It’s definitely cyber attacks by the Chinese” — as they begin to search for a Chinese spy who must be living in South America.

But Butters has known the billionaires were to blame since the beginning, and confides in Stan, Kyle and Cartman. Oh, and a zombified Kenny (who got blown up by an e-bike during the season’s premiere) also makes a few appearances. Turns out, Kenny’s spirit can’t move on until the group figures out what’s going on and exposes all the corruption in South America — great timing for them, as they’ve all learned about the data centers and are ready to make a change. The gang takes this revelation to City Hall, where the politicians explain how it all began “with those damn e-bikes” and fear that the group of kids is going to blow their secret deal with the billionaires.

But it wasn’t an e-bike that killed Kenny, says a still shrunken Randy who barges into City Hall. “We all did. We all share in the blame equally… When they changed the name of our town, we let it change us. We used force, took bribes, looked the other way, because we let them change South Park.”

In the end, the mayor of South American addresses the town and owns up to her mistakes — kind of.

“I owe you all an apology. Since the town changed its name, I’ve taken bribes. I’ve looked the other way, and I did make a deal with billionaires,” she says. “But I’ve learned that even though we must build data centers because there is a very real threat from the Chinese in the AI arms race, we can’t let that change who we are.”

And so, the billionaire weenies help the town “do something we should have done a long time ago”: shoot Mr. Kim into space and out of South America. And with that, another season of “South Park” (and the first season with the new name of “South America”) comes to a close.

Jonathan Nolan's 103-Episode Sci-Fi Series Is Westworld But Better

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jonathan Nolan has enjoyed a hugely successful career, helping shape some of modern sci-fi’s most ambitious films and TV shows. However, among his many achievements sits a particularly frustrating misfire: HBO’s Westworld. Its spectacular opening season set up a show that felt capable of redefining science-fiction on the small screen, pairing blockbuster production values with deep thematic questions about the very nature of consciousness. Unfortunately, by the time Westworld finished, it had become synonymous with the idea of TV series that start strong but quickly fell off.

Westworld’s journey from being touted as the next great pop-culture obsession to a series many viewers would rather forget is now infamous. Fortunately, Jonathan Nolan’s filmography contains another show that tackles similar ideas while becoming more, not less, rewarding as it goes on. The specifics of Nolan's Person of Interest may not feel much like Westworld, but beneath the differences explores many of the same ideas about artificial intelligence, human freedom, and the responsibilities of creation.

Airing from 2011 to 2016 on CBS, Person of Interest is Jonathan Nolan's 103-episode saga of former CIA operative John Reese (Jim Caviezel), billionaire Harold Finch (Michael Emerson), and the advanced surveillance system, the Machine, that they use to identify people connected to imminent violent crimes. From this premise, Person of Interest gradually expands from a crime procedural with a technological twist into a gripping and deep exploration of humanity’s relationship with increasingly powerful technology. For Westworld fans still wishing that the HBO show's extraordinary beginning had delivered lasting greatness, Person of Interest is the answer.

While a crime procedural about anticipating crimes before they happen and a drama about a wild west theme park full of synthetic beings are wildly different, Jonathan Nolan’s Person of Interest and Westworld share a fascination with how humanity treats the intelligent beings it feels inevitable that it will one day create. Both series question whether artificial consciousness deserves compassion and whether its creators have any right to control it. Person of Interest explores these concerns through Finch’s relationship with the Machine. His efforts to give it moral boundaries become an ongoing examination of the difference between teaching something to care and demanding its obedience.

The procedural format of Person of Interest means the series doesn’t have to waste time diving into these ideas. Every episode can explore the same concept from a new angle with a self-contained story. Each person the team tries to save reinforces the importance of an individual life, making the Machine’s developing understanding of humanity feel earned. When the story eventually expands into competing visions of an AI-controlled future and a more serialized long-form narrative, viewers understand the risks of an advanced AI system that sees human beings as data. They also have a firm grasp of whose responsibility it is to ensure this doesn’t happen.

That gives Person of Interest a firmer emotional foundation than Westworld managed to create. Its arguments about the relationship between security and freedom in a world where technology is advancing at an exponential speed emerge through decisions made by characters that viewers have been on dozens of episodic adventures with. The show’s escalation from its crime-procedural roots in later seasons feels like the premise reaching its full potential rather than being forced. By the time Person of Interest starts asking viewers to think bigger, they’re more than ready.

It’s a stark contrast to Westworld, whose story was so complex that understanding it left little room to try and digest its core philosophical message. The HBO series lost itself within an overly convoluted mystery box. It had some great and intriguing ideas, but few were willing to stick around to discover them. Person of Interest certainly has its mysteries, but its central themes remain clear even as its sci-fi became more ambitious. For viewers disappointed by Westworld’s trajectory, that combination of intellectual ambition and emotional clarity makes it an exceptionally satisfying alternative.

Having Person of Interest available to satisfy the appetite for thoughtful AI-focused storytelling that Westworld should have done is welcome, but the comparison is bittersweet. There are very few out there who would say Westworld is a great show from start to finish, but it easily could have been if it lived up to its incredible first seasons throughout its run. The HBO show created enormous expectations for itself when it first arrived. Its luxurious cinematography, disturbing premise, and extraordinary cast performances made it feel it was poised to be a hit on the scale of the likes of Lost or Game of Thrones.

Westworld’s premise alone drew immediate attention. The idea of a playground populated by sentient but unaware AI that wealthy guests could indulge their worst impulses against was a strong hook, and one that had obvious potential for something bigger. The first season did a great job of taking this foundation and creating a story and characters viewers cared about. The anticipation of discovering how its elements connected and where the wider narrative would take the premise felt like it offered more than the satisfaction of solving a mystery.

The park itself was also key to why season 1 of Westworld was so good. It gave viewers a tangible and unique setting within which to explore the show’s ideas. Scripted conversations, repeated routines, and the contrast between the western landscape and clinical backstage facilities made the questions about free will feel worth answering. The show barely needed to explain why the arrangement was horrifying and why finding a new status quo mattered on an emotional level. The initial worldbuilding did all the work without requiring heavy-handed exposition. Despite the complexity of its ideas, Westworld’s early chapters had a clear sense of purpose.

Had Westworld sustained that balance and momentum after season 1, it would probably rank among the greatest science fiction shows ever made. At its peak, its cinematic scale and emotional force offered a jaw-dropping experience that inarguably outshined Person of Interest’s comparatively modest beginnings. Westworld’s decline isn’t just disappointing, it’s actually frustrating when thinking about how good it could have been.

Fidelity Files for Chainlink ETF. What Does It Mean for LINK?

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Fidelity’s Chainlink ETF proposal would hold LINK directly and trade on Nasdaq if it clears the remaining steps. The proposed Fidelity Chainlink Fund, described in a November 25 registration statement, would use the ticker FLNK, although the filing is preliminary and no launch date or final annual fee has been provided.

The larger question is not whether Wall Street can package LINK. It already has. The question is whether Fidelity can attract fresh demand for the token rather than divide the existing market among another set of fund shares.

The proposed fund would hold LINK, not a basket of blockchain companies or derivatives intended to mimic LINK’s price. Its objective is to follow the token’s U.S. dollar performance, less expenses and liabilities. The prospectus identifies Anchorage Digital Bank, BitGo Bank & Trust and Coinbase Custody as custodians, with the trust intending to hold its LINK on Ethereum.

Fidelity also lays out how the price would be measured. Its proposed reference rate uses prices from eligible LINK spot markets, with a volume-weighted median methodology calculated every 15 seconds using rolling 60-minute market data. That is a mechanism for valuing the assets. It is not a guarantee that fund shares will trade at precisely the reported value when markets are thin or volatile.

Nor is this a yield product. The filing states plainly: “The Trust will not stake its LINK.” Shareholders would not receive staking rewards through the fund. They would be buying securities designed to reflect an underlying token’s price, not a wallet balance they can use in Chainlink services.

That distinction matters because the company’s use of Chainlink technology elsewhere is a separate story. NewsBTC has previously reported on Chainlink infrastructure used with Fidelity’s tokenized FILQ fund. An investment vehicle that owns LINK is not evidence of increased demand for Chainlink’s oracle services, and using the network’s technology does not automatically mean buying its token.

This is not the first attempt to make LINK available through conventional securities accounts. Grayscale’s Chainlink Trust ETF, trading as GLNK, had a share creation and redemption program in place from December 2025, according to its SEC disclosures. The Bitwise Chainlink ETF, CLNK, also appeared in a January 2026 Nasdaq information circular.

And fund shares are not the only conventional route. In November, NewsBTC covered Charles Schwab’s rollout of direct LINK trading to eligible crypto clients. These routes do different things, but they establish a useful baseline: access is no longer the same problem it was when crypto products were confined to specialist exchanges.

Fidelity’s possible advantage is distribution. Its name and brokerage relationships may reach customers who would not open a crypto wallet or buy LINK on a trading platform. That is a credible reason the filing matters. But branding, however recognizable, cannot tell investors how much money will arrive once trading begins.

There is also the price of access. The preliminary prospectus leaves the sponsor’s annual fee blank. Until that figure is settled, it is impossible to make an honest cost comparison with competing LINK products. For investors choosing between wrappers around the same token, fees, trading spreads and the gap between share prices and net asset value deserve more attention than a new ticker.

The mechanics are worth getting right. The proposed trust would issue and redeem large blocks of shares through authorized participants, using cash or LINK under procedures described in the filing. Everyday investors would generally trade the shares on an exchange instead of placing orders with the trust.

Those are two different markets. When one investor sells an existing fund share to another, the trade does not by itself require the trust to acquire more LINK. Sustained net creations can increase a fund’s underlying holdings. Redemptions can do the reverse. A rising share price, meanwhile, may reflect a higher LINK price rather than additional tokens entering custody.

That is why the announcement alone provides no evidence of fresh institutional inflows. The Form S-1 is a plan, not a subscription report. It does not tell us how much outside capital will be committed, what portion might come from rival products, or whether investor interest will survive a difficult crypto market.

There is a fair counterargument. Fidelity could appeal to a materially different pool of investors and grow total demand rather than simply redistribute it. The filing establishes a possible channel for that to happen. It does not establish the outcome.

First, the registration statement must become effective and the proposed Nasdaq listing must move forward. The final prospectus should also resolve the missing fee and other commercial details. Those are immediate milestones, not formalities to be assumed away.

If the product launches, the more revealing evidence will arrive afterward: the number of LINK held, changes in shares outstanding, sustained net creations or redemptions, trading liquidity and discounts or premiums to net asset value. Comparisons with existing LINK funds would help separate fresh demand from investors moving between products.

Until those figures exist, the sensible interpretation is narrow. Fidelity wants to sell investors exposure to Chainlink through a familiar brokerage format. Whether that changes the market for LINK is still an open question, and one that a filing cannot answer.

‘South Park’ Celebrates ‘GTA VI’ But Outages, AI Data Centers & Zombie Kenny Stand In The Way In Season 29 Finale

📅 Nov 26, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As the long wait for Grand Theft Auto VI finally ends, South Park‘s celebration was cut short on this week’s episode.

In the finale “City Endings,” outages hit the town (currently rebranded as South America for Season 29) thanks to the AI data centers of Billionaire Weenietown that moved into Butters’ backyard after Mayor McDaniels cut a deal to bring them to town.

A mob visits the mayor, who instead of taking responsibility, leads them to believe the outages are “definitely cyber attacks by the Chinese.” That prompts the mob to stake out Mr. Kim’s City Parlor, where they each end up getting a massage with a “city ending.” Watch on Deadline

Meanwhile, Kyle, Stan and Cartman are visited by the ghost of Kenny, who previously died when a bomb strapped to his e-bike exploded.

The trio gives Butters a visit after remembering his connectivity issues while trying to watch Dr. Pimple Popper videos. It turns out, Butters is no longer affected by the outages as he’s since been getting some kickbacks from Billionaire Weenietown, including a massive GTA VI setup.

In the middle of Butters’ gameplay, zombie Kenny appears on the screen, breaking out like Samara in The Ring to deliver a message to his friends.

The episode culminates with the kids confronting the cops and the mayor, only to find out they were all complicit in Kenny’s death when they allowed the billionaires to come to South Park.

Ultimately, Mayor McDaniels takes responsibility for the billionaires ruining the town but sends Mr. Kim to space on WeenieX in order to appease the town. The show ends with “America” falling off the South Park sign after they rolled over to change the town’s name for Trump.

South Park‘s finale wrapped up its Billionaire Weenietown storyline with some more topical jabs, following the massively unprecedented release of GTA VI, as well as the ongoing nationwide outages, which the government has chalked up to many networks changing over to AI systems.

RoboSense LiDAR Sales Surge as Robotics and AI Sensing Expand

📅 Nov 25, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

RoboSense reported on November 25, 2026 that unaudited third-quarter 2026 revenue rose 65.2% year over year to RMB672.7 million, while LiDAR sales volume climbed 210.1% to approximately 575,500 units. Space Camera also entered the supply chains of several global AI companies, a separate sensing development alongside the LiDAR shipment growth.

RoboSense reported approximately 427,800 LiDAR units for robotics and other applications in the first nine months of 2026, up 423.0% year over year. Its digital LiDAR is being used across commercial cleaning, autonomous delivery, embodied AI and 3D spatial data collection. The range of applications ties the increase to several end uses rather than a single robotics category.

Integration of E2 and EM4 with NVIDIA Holoscan Sensor Bridge expands the development ecosystem around those sensors. The company’s reliability and integration work is aimed at customers moving robots into practical deployments, with its existing robotics customer base providing a route to commercialize newer products.

ADAS remains the larger disclosed category by units: RoboSense reported approximately 866,900 LiDAR sales for advanced driver-assistance systems in the nine-month period. As of September 30, 2026, its design wins covered 197 vehicle models with 36 automotive OEMs and Tier 1 suppliers, and 91 of those models had reached start of production. The automotive base therefore remains important to the company’s scale even as robotics volumes expand rapidly.

Space Camera is being validated with AI model companies and data-collection partners for egocentric data capture and motion capture. For humanoid robots, the RGB-D camera supplies spatial perception for mobility and dexterous manipulation.

RoboSense said it is collaborating with AI model developers in North America and humanoid robotics companies, that its products have received testing validation certificates from several customers, and that shared perception across data collection and robot operation can help close the gap between model training and real-world deployment.

The first vehicle model equipped with RoboSense’s Phoenix SPAD-SoC chip entered mass production and delivery in November 2026. Also that month, the company commissioned a Shenzhen digital LiDAR line with reported annual capacity of 500,000 units and six operators at final assembly. Together, the chip and production line connect the shipment expansion to RoboSense’s in-house semiconductor and automotive-grade manufacturing capabilities.

RoboSense expects fourth-quarter 2026 LiDAR sales volume to exceed one million units. That target makes production scale and sensor integration the immediate test: the company has reported rapid shipment growth and added capacity, and the next results will show whether those developments support another substantial increase.
